# Guidance traceability

Every product rule is listed here with the automated checks that enforce it. `tests/test_guidance.py` fails if a
referenced test or CI step disappears, so a rule cannot lose its check without this table changing in the same review.

Reference formats: `tests/<file>.py::<test>` for a pytest test, and `ci:<step or job name>` for a GitHub Actions step
or job.

| ID | Rule | Enforced by |
|---|---|---|
| G01 | Offline by default: no web, no cluster, no telemetry unless enabled | `tests/test_config_net.py::test_defaults_are_offline`, `tests/test_debug.py::test_cluster_disabled_by_default`, `tests/test_server_agent_cli.py::test_toolkit_availability_gates` |
| G02 | Admin offline lock overrides `webEnabled`, blocks corpus downloads, allows only local models, and only tightens | `tests/test_offline_lock.py::test_lock_beats_web_enabled_everywhere`, `tests/test_offline_lock.py::test_lock_allows_only_local_models`, `tests/test_offline_lock.py::test_lock_only_tightens`, `ci:Chart guards (fail-closed NetworkPolicy, fsGroup only off-OpenShift, web flag wired to env)`, `tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_any_true_spelling_locks`, `tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_unreadable_system_config_fails_closed`, `tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_locked_model_client_never_leaves_loopback`, `tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_lock_locality_is_strict`, `tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_corpus_download_is_refused_by_the_lock_itself` |
| G03 | Web, when enabled: HTTPS GET only, allowlisted domains and redirects, text content only, size-capped, never written to disk or executed | `tests/test_offline_lock.py::test_web_reads_text_only_and_only_with_get`, `tests/test_config_net.py::test_domain_allowlist_applies_to_redirects`, `tests/test_config_net.py::test_web_blocked_when_disabled`, `tests/test_security_hardening.py::test_web_fetch_stops_at_max_bytes`, `tests/test_offline_lock.py::test_web_fetch_bounds_compressed_bodies_and_odd_charsets` |
| G04 | Posture is provable: `doctor` reports reach and fails on contradictions | `tests/test_offline_lock.py::test_doctor_reports_posture_and_flags_conflicts` |
| G05 | Cluster access is read-only, Secrets are never read, values are redacted | `tests/test_debug.py::test_secrets_never_allowed`, `tests/test_debug.py::test_redaction`, `tests/test_security_hardening.py::test_cluster_redaction_covers_values_args_and_list_wrappers`, `ci:Helm test (in-cluster readiness through the Service)` |
| G06 | Diagnose before change: read-only steps (get, describe, logs, bundle) first; exec and changes need approval | `tests/test_diagnose_first.py::test_classify_step`, `tests/test_diagnose_first.py::test_playbook_checks_never_mutate`, `tests/test_diagnose_first.py::test_diagnosis_orders_read_only_steps_first_and_gates_the_rest`, `tests/test_diagnose_first.py::test_bundle_writes_redacted_read_only_evidence`, `tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_classify_segments`, `tests/test_diagnose_first.py::test_diagnose_cli_takes_pasted_text_a_file_or_stdin` |
| G07 | Commands meant for copy-paste never carry shell comments (zsh would pass them as arguments) | `tests/test_diagnose_first.py::test_no_copyable_command_carries_a_shell_comment`, `tests/test_diagnose_first.py::test_split_comment`, `tests/test_review_regressions.py::test_every_playbook_command_is_paste_safe` |

| G40 | A memory entry is a reviewed summary, not a dump: prose rejects credentials, raw logs, event rows, stack traces, pipelines and manifests; each command is one line with no shell comment, carries the classifier's label (read-only or needs-approval) and never prints a Secret; a patch is manifests without Secrets or credentials; evidence is a reference only; the file is bounded, has one document, no duplicate keys and is named after its id; the shipped JSON Schema matches the model | `tests/test_memory_entry.py::test_bad_entries_are_rejected_with_a_reason`, `tests/test_memory_entry.py::test_commands_carry_the_classifiers_label_and_nothing_else`, `tests/test_memory_entry.py::test_patches_are_manifests_without_secrets`, `tests/test_memory_entry.py::test_yaml_level_attacks_are_refused`, `tests/test_memory_entry.py::test_the_file_name_must_match_the_id`, `tests/test_memory_entry.py::test_round_trip_is_exact_and_deterministic`, `tests/test_memory_entry.py::test_the_shipped_json_schema_matches_the_model_and_checks_shape` |

| G118 | SAFE2: a shared HTTP server records no learner progress. `learn_lesson`, `learn_submit` and `learn_quiz` take a `learner` id, and the server has one static bearer token, so that id comes from the caller and binds to no identity: any caller could write and read any other's tally, and concurrent answers lost each other. | G121 | Bundled YAML is written so that every conformant parser reads it the same way: no colon inside an unquoted scalar in a flow collection, and no `key: value` pair sitting directly in a `[ ]` sequence. Both are ambiguous, and PyYAML's own scanner accepts what a stricter libyaml refuses, so a file that parses in CI can break a shipped install. The check reads the scanner's tokens rather than trusting whichever parser is installed, and covers `.yaml`, `.yml` and the front matter of knowledge documents, in `src/`, the plugin copy and `examples/`. The Ceph catalogue, which `diagnose` and `known_issues` both load, falls back to PyYAML's scanner rather than raising if libyaml ever objects | `tests/test_distribution.py::test_bundled_yaml_parses_under_every_yaml_parser`, `tests/test_ceph_health.py::test_ceph_status_summaries_map_to_codes` |

A blank environment variable counts as unset rather than as a setting of "", so a shell that exports one unconditionally cannot stop the server starting | `tests/test_distribution.py::test_the_plugin_asks_the_user_for_nothing_and_reads_nothing_from_their_machine`, `tests/test_plugin.py::test_mcp_config_runs_the_bundled_source_from_the_lock`, `tests/test_mustgather_mcp.py::test_a_blank_variable_is_an_unset_one_not_a_refused_start` |
| G123 | Pasted JSON is refused on its bracket count before it is parsed, so the refusal does not depend on the interpreter: `json.loads` raises `RecursionError` up to CPython 3.13 but relies on the C stack from 3.14, where a deep paste overflows the WebAssembly stack as a fatal `RangeError` and took `diagnose` and `known_issues` down in the browser. An operator's must-gather file that only one of the two YAML parsers accepts is read by that one, instead of being recorded as unparsed and quietly dropping a finding past a `--fail-on` gate | `tests/test_red_team_rt1.py::test_deep_json_is_refused_by_counting_brackets_not_by_recursing`, `tests/test_red_team_rt1.py::test_deeply_nested_jsonl_cases_are_refused_cleanly`, `tests/test_mustgather_analyse.py::test_a_file_only_one_yaml_parser_accepts_is_still_read` |
| G124 | A must-gather analysis says what it could not see, and a gate can refuse to read a blind run as a clean one: `completeness` reports the analysers that had data, the files that could not be read and whether the evidence is whole; the text output says so in words; and `--require-complete` exits 5, distinct from 1 (findings at or above `--fail-on`) and from 2, 3 and 4 (config, clarification, refused input). Without the flag nothing changes, so no existing gate shifts under anyone | `tests/test_mustgather_analyse.py::test_a_blind_run_is_not_reported_as_a_clean_one` |
| G125 | The API key the model client sends is only ever the one named for that provider: `apiKeyEnv` when set, else the provider's own default, and only `anthropic` has one. An `openai-compatible` endpoint - any gateway the user named - therefore never receives `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` by default, and `apiKeyEnv` may only name a `*_API_KEY` variable, so a configuration file cannot point the model client at `KUBE_NOOB_HTTP_TOKEN` (the MCP server's own bearer token, held by the same process). Error bodies from an endpoint are redacted before they are quoted, so a gateway that echoes the `Authorization` header in a 400 cannot put the key into stderr, logs or CI output; `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` from the environment is held to the same clear-text rule as `llm.baseUrl`; and a 200 whose body is not the documented shape, or a redirect loop, ends in a message rather than a traceback | `tests/test_red_team_apikey.py::test_the_anthropic_key_is_never_the_default_for_another_endpoint`, `tests/test_red_team_apikey.py::test_an_echoed_key_is_redacted_from_the_error`, `tests/test_red_team_apikey.py::test_apikeyenv_may_not_name_a_non_key_variable`, `tests/test_red_team_apikey.py::test_the_cleartext_guard_covers_the_endpoint_from_the_environment`, `tests/test_red_team_apikey.py::test_an_unexpected_response_shape_is_an_error_not_a_traceback`, `tests/test_red_team_apikey.py::test_a_redirect_loop_is_an_error_not_a_traceback` |
| G126 | A directory of manifests is read one file at a time, so a file nobody can read is named and counted rather than deciding the outcome for every other file: `audit -f DIR` and `gate -f DIR` used to concatenate the whole tree before anything parsed it, so one file that was not UTF-8 or not YAML aborted the audit of the rest and reported a line of the combined text, which is no line of any file the user can open. Unreadable files now exit 5, distinct from 1 (findings at or above `--fail-on`), because a `gate` that crashed on a NUL byte used to leave 1 and a pipeline read that as policy violations; `--skip-unreadable` audits the rest and lists them under `unreadable` in the report, and `--fail-on` still bites on the files that were read | `tests/test_red_team_rt3.py::test_a_crash_never_wears_the_exit_code_of_a_policy_failure`, `tests/test_red_team_rt3.py::test_an_unreadable_file_is_named_not_located_in_a_concatenation`, `tests/test_red_team_rt3.py::test_one_bad_file_does_not_hide_the_findings_in_the_good_ones`, `tests/test_red_team_rt3.py::test_skip_unreadable_still_fails_on_severity`, `tests/test_red_team_rt3.py::test_a_clean_directory_is_unchanged` |
| G127 | A reachability verdict evaluates all three network policy tiers, because the two cluster-scoped ones do not narrow the answer, they change it: an `AdminNetworkPolicy` `Deny` blocks traffic every NetworkPolicy in the namespace allows, an `Allow` permits traffic every NetworkPolicy denies, and a `BaselineAdminNetworkPolicy` decides the traffic no NetworkPolicy selects. Order is ANP (by `priority` ascending, then rule order, first match wins; `Pass` ends the tier), then NetworkPolicy -- whose answer is final where it selects the pod -- then BANP, whose no-match is an allow. A manifest the API server would itself refuse (a priority outside 0-1000, a BANP not named `default`, an action that is not one of the API's) is reported as a mistake rather than evaluated or guessed at, an `Allow`/`Deny` above OVN-Kubernetes's priority 99 says that the cluster ignores it, and a `nodes` or `networks` peer says it is not evaluated offline. The live check reads both tiers once, cluster-scoped, and a cluster without the CRDs is a note rather than a failure | `tests/test_netpol_anp.py::test_an_admin_deny_beats_a_networkpolicy_that_allows_everything`, `tests/test_netpol_anp.py::test_an_admin_allow_beats_a_networkpolicy_that_denies_everything`, `tests/test_netpol_anp.py::test_pass_hands_the_decision_back_to_the_namespace`, `tests/test_netpol_anp.py::test_the_lowest_priority_number_decides`, `tests/test_netpol_anp.py::test_the_baseline_decides_only_what_no_networkpolicy_selects`, `tests/test_netpol_anp.py::test_a_baseline_not_named_default_is_reported_not_applied`, `tests/test_netpol_anp.py::test_an_unknown_action_is_reported_and_not_guessed`, `tests/test_netpol_anp.py::test_the_live_check_reads_the_admin_tiers_too`, `tests/test_netpol_anp.py::test_a_cluster_that_has_no_admin_tier_api_is_not_an_error`, `tests/test_netpol_anp.py::test_it_does_not_propose_a_change_that_the_admin_tier_would_still_block`, `tests/test_safety_contract.py::test_the_chart_read_rules_mirror_the_backends_allowlist` |
| G128 | The HTTP server's guards run before the work they protect and on the bytes the work actually reads: a body on `/healthz` or `/readyz` is refused with 405 before it is buffered (those are the only paths an unauthenticated caller reaches, and buffering there let anything that could open the port take the pod to its memory limit); the body limit is 1.5 MiB rather than 8 MiB, since every tool argument is capped at 1,000,000 characters so a larger body can never be valid, and each buffered body is copied again and bracket-scanned in pure Python; the JSON nesting guard counts the one decoded string the parser is given, because `json.loads` sniffs UTF-16 and UTF-32 and a backslash before a NUL byte desynchronised a guard that read a UTF-8 decoding of the same bytes; two `Authorization` headers are refused rather than resolved last-wins; and the content-free audit line truncates argument names, which arrive unvalidated from the caller on the REST path | `tests/test_red_team_rt4.py::test_an_unauthenticated_caller_cannot_make_the_server_buffer_a_body`, `tests/test_red_team_rt4.py::test_the_body_limit_is_not_larger_than_a_valid_request`, `tests/test_red_team_rt4.py::test_the_guard_counted_a_different_string_from_the_one_the_parser_read`, `tests/test_red_team_rt4.py::test_the_nesting_guard_reads_the_same_bytes_the_parser_does`, `tests/test_red_team_rt4.py::test_a_valid_utf16_body_is_still_understood`, `tests/test_red_team_rt4.py::test_a_duplicate_authorization_header_is_refused`, `tests/test_red_team_rt4.py::test_the_audit_line_does_not_log_what_the_caller_chose_to_send` |
| G129 | The knowledge index is cached on the content that feeds it, not on the file that holds it: `learning.json` contributes the team's confirmed notes as searchable chunks, but almost every tool call rewrites it to bump a counter, so keying the cache on its size and mtime rebuilt the whole index on nearly every request -- about fourteen times slower on the shared HTTP server. The key covers the notes themselves, so a new note is searchable at once, a counter costs nothing, and a file that cannot be read as notes falls back to its stat rather than serving a stale index | `tests/test_red_team_rt4.py::test_the_knowledge_store_is_not_rebuilt_because_a_counter_moved` |
| G130 | A burst of connections that send nothing starves the HTTP server's pool -- uvicorn counts connections towards `limit_concurrency` and nothing reclaims one that never sent a request, so every request including the kubelet's probes answers 503 until they close (measured: 70 idle sockets, still 503 after 16s). kube-noob cannot close that in process, so what it owns stays consistent instead: buffered-body memory is the concurrency limit times the body limit and has to leave the container room to run (64 x 1.5 MiB against 512Mi, where it used to be 64 x 8 MiB -- exactly the ceiling the kernel kills at); the liveness probe is four times more forgiving than readiness, so sustained overload sheds traffic rather than restarting a pod whose restart frees no connection; and SECURITY.md says the port belongs behind a proxy that enforces a header-read timeout | `tests/test_red_team_rt6.py::test_the_concurrency_limit_and_the_body_limit_stay_consistent`, `tests/test_red_team_rt6.py::test_overload_sheds_traffic_rather_than_restarting_the_pod`, `tests/test_red_team_rt6.py::test_the_security_policy_states_the_limitation` |
| G131 | The release job attaches its assets whether or not the release already exists. `gh release create` is an error on an existing release, and a maintainer creating one by hand -- to write their own notes, or because a tag push looked like it did nothing -- is ordinary: on v0.7.1 it failed `github-release` and skipped `videos` and `site` behind it, so the release carried no wheel, no chart and no videos, and kube-noob.com was never told the version had changed, while PyPI, the chart repository, the MCP registry and Homebrew had all published. The step branches on `gh release view` and uploads with `--clobber`, so a re-run replaces an asset instead of failing on it | `tests/test_distribution.py::test_the_release_job_survives_a_release_the_maintainer_made_first` |
| G132 | The plugin declares exactly the dependencies the bundled server imports, checked both ways: a module imported but not declared is an ImportError on the user's first call, because uv runs the server `--frozen --exact --isolated` and nothing ambient fills the gap; a dependency declared but never imported is a package the user installs for nothing and a reviewer has to account for. The plugin had declared `httpx` and `uvicorn`, which belong to the web client and the HTTP service and ship in neither, and left `anyio`, `mcp-types` and `pydantic-core` to arrive through another package's requirements although it imports all three directly. Dropping the two removed `httpx`, `httpcore` and `certifi` from the resolved tree, so the installed set holds no HTTP client at all. The launcher stays the vendored one and the README says why, rather than promising a switch to a published package that was reverted before the release | `tests/test_plugin.py::test_the_plugin_declares_what_it_imports_and_imports_what_it_declares`, `tests/test_plugin.py::test_lock_pins_every_dependency_with_registry_and_hashes`, `tests/test_plugin.py::test_mcp_config_runs_the_bundled_source_from_the_lock` |
| G133 | The audit refuses what it cannot audit instead of scoring it. Given a Helm values file it used to read the whole document as one unnamed object, report that `apiVersion` was missing, and return a score -- 90 out of 100 for a file whose images were `:latest` and whose `securityContext` and `resources` were empty. A score says the rules were applied, so a score on a file the rules never saw is a wrong answer rather than a thin one. With no document carrying both `apiVersion` and `kind`, `audit`, `validate` and `redteam` exit 2 and name what the file looks like and the command that would make it auditable; one Kubernetes document anywhere in the input is enough to proceed, so a values block pasted above a manifest does not refuse the manifest | `tests/test_red_team_rt7.py::test_a_file_that_is_not_a_manifest_is_not_scored`, `tests/test_red_team_rt7.py::test_it_names_what_the_file_looks_like`, `tests/test_red_team_rt7.py::test_a_manifest_beside_a_values_file_still_audits`, `tests/test_red_team_rt7.py::test_the_toolkit_says_the_same_thing_as_the_cli` |
| G134 | kube-noob's knowledge answers questions about kube-noob. The corpus held nothing about the tool itself, so a client asked for its CI story got Kyverno and RHACS documents, wrote `kube-noob audit -f rendered.yaml  # check the CLI's --help for the flag`, and never mentioned `gate` -- the command built for that job. `kube-noob-cli.md` carries `gate` and its CI outputs, the exit codes, the `ExceptionList` schema, what the audit does and does not read, and the command table; tests hold its exit codes to the CLI and run its exception example through the real loader, so it cannot drift into teaching the wrong thing with a citation attached | `tests/test_red_team_rt7.py::test_a_client_can_find_out_what_kube_noob_itself_can_do`, `tests/test_red_team_rt7.py::test_the_document_tells_the_truth_about_the_exit_codes`, `tests/test_red_team_rt7.py::test_the_exception_example_in_the_document_actually_loads` |
| G135 | The package points at the website and the README leads with what only kube-noob does. Nothing linked kube-noob.com -- not `project.urls`, not the README, not the plugin manifest, not the chart -- so the demos, recordings and browsable rules were reachable only by already knowing the address, while the README opened with a five-bullet feature list that buried the two questions no other tool answers: which tier of `AdminNetworkPolicy`/`NetworkPolicy`/`BaselineAdminNetworkPolicy` governs a connection, and which of six admission systems denied a pod. Both now open the README with a runnable command each, against an example that ships | `tests/test_distribution.py::test_the_package_points_at_the_website`, `tests/test_guidance.py::test_docs_are_concise_and_linked`, `tests/test_guidance.py::test_readme_numbers_match_the_code` |
| G136 | Documentation does not promise an artefact that has never been published. `docs/use-cases.md` told readers the public `kube-noob-db` image is "rebuilt daily" and gave them cosign and helm commands for it, while the `db-image` workflow has failed on every scheduled run since it was added and has published nothing. A reader spends their time before they find out, so the page now says it is not published, and gives the two commands that do exist (`corpus db-bundle`, `corpus db-check`) for building one themselves | `tests/test_distribution.py::test_nothing_promises_an_artefact_that_has_never_published` |
| G137 | A health signal reports on the thing a person cares about, not on something next to it. Two did not: `route.enabled: true` with no `route.host` let OpenShift generate a hostname the chart cannot predict and so never puts in `server.allowedHosts`, giving **421 Invalid Host header** on every MCP call while both probes and `helm test` stayed green -- the chart now refuses to render and says how to get the domain; and `kube-noob doctor` printed `posture: OK` with a provider configured, no SDK installed and no key set, while its own help promises "exit 1 on conflicts" -- it now names the missing SDK or the unset variable and exits 1. The check lives in `cli.py` rather than `posture.py` because the latter ships in the plugin, which must name no credential variable for a reviewer. The guard then found the same defect in our own CI, which had been rendering the Route with no host since it was added | `tests/test_red_team_rt8.py::test_no_exposure_can_create_a_hostname_the_server_will_refuse`, `tests/test_red_team_rt8.py::test_the_route_guard_says_what_to_do`, `tests/test_red_team_rt8.py::test_doctor_does_not_call_a_model_path_healthy_that_cannot_run`, `tests/test_red_team_rt8.py::test_doctor_is_quiet_when_no_model_is_configured`, `tests/test_red_team_rt8.py::test_our_own_workflows_do_not_render_the_configuration_the_chart_refuses` |
| G138 | A command in the README is a promise, so every path it passes must either ship or read as the reader's own. `kube-noob policy explain -f denial.txt` shipped no `denial.txt`, so the first command a stranger copied answered `error: no such file`. It now points at `examples/denial-multi.txt`, where five admission systems reject one Deployment in one paste, and `-f k8s/` became `-f your-manifests/`. A claim about a command is the same promise: the README said both policy commands exit non-zero for CI, and only `reach` does -- correct the claim, not the code, when the code is right | `tests/test_guidance.py::test_every_file_the_readme_tells_a_stranger_to_run_exists` |
| G139 | When an input contains something that outranks what was evaluated, the answer names it and the proposed fix says so. `ClusterNetworkPolicy` (`policy.networking.k8s.io/v1alpha2`, the API still under active development; the v1alpha1 ANP/BANP pair kube-noob evaluates are upstream's "no longer actively developed") was not a kind kube-noob knew, so a cluster-scoped Admin-tier Deny vanished from the answer and `policy reach` proposed a NetworkPolicy the admin tier overrides. Unknown-but-outranking is not the same as absent | `tests/test_netpol_cnp_guard.py::test_a_clusternetworkpolicy_decides_the_answer`, `tests/test_netpol_cnp_guard.py::test_the_fix_for_a_cluster_scoped_deny_is_not_a_networkpolicy`, `tests/test_netpol_cnp_guard.py::test_the_answer_is_printed_where_the_reader_reads_it`, `tests/test_netpol_cnp_guard.py::test_no_caveat_when_nothing_cluster_scoped_is_in_the_input` |
| G140 | What a public repository publishes is permanent: a fork or an archive keeps it after the commit that removes it. An instruction names a placeholder rather than a real address, and a private working file stays out of git rather than relying on nobody reading it. `docs/openshift-release.md` carried the maintainer's address inside a `git config` command, and `.claude/loop/` published the unreleased backlog. Commit metadata is the exception: rewriting published history costs more than the exposure | `tests/test_distribution.py::test_no_real_address_is_published_in_the_repository`, `tests/test_distribution.py::test_the_private_build_queue_is_not_published` |
| G141 | A job that cannot do its work fails. Warning and exiting 0 because a secret is missing puts the news in an annotation nobody opens and the word SUCCESS where a person looks, which is how a release finished green having never told the website about itself. A guard may exit 0 only when the run still does the work another way, and it has to say which way. Comparisons that decide pass or fail compare values, not strings: `site-sync` used equality, so a site AHEAD of the latest release read as a failed deploy | `tests/test_workflow_honesty.py::test_no_job_exits_zero_because_a_secret_it_needs_is_missing`, `tests/test_workflow_honesty.py::test_the_drift_check_does_not_call_a_site_that_is_ahead_a_failure` |
| G142 | A prose claim about a machine-readable file is read against that file by a test, or it drifts and then it is a lie someone can check. The plugin README said the installed tree had no HTTP client and no web server and "cannot reach the network", while the `uv.lock` beside it shipped `httpx2`, `httpcore2`, `h11`, `uvicorn` and `sse-starlette` -- published on the site and sent to a reviewer who can open the lock. State what the bundled code does, which is checkable, not what the dependency tree lacks | `tests/test_plugin_readme_truth.py::test_the_readme_states_the_real_number_of_locked_packages`, `tests/test_plugin_readme_truth.py::test_the_readme_does_not_deny_a_dependency_the_lock_file_contains`, `tests/test_plugin_readme_truth.py::test_every_package_the_readme_names_is_in_the_lock` |
| G143 | A fix is checked against the thing it set out to fix, not against the mechanism it changed. `doctor` gained a conflict list and an exit code and still printed `posture: OK`, because the status field was computed earlier; the test asserted the list and the code, so it passed. Assert the signal a person reads | `tests/test_red_team_rt8.py::test_the_posture_header_says_conflict_when_there_is_one`, `tests/test_red_team_rt8.py::test_the_posture_header_is_rendered_from_the_same_status` |
| G144 | A new check must not refuse a configuration the code documents as supported. `openai-compatible` with no `apiKeyEnv` deliberately sends no Authorization header and is the only provider the offline lock allows; flagging a missing key variable without asking whether the provider has a default one broke it | `tests/test_red_team_rt8.py::test_a_provider_with_no_default_key_variable_is_not_a_conflict`, `tests/test_red_team_rt8.py::test_a_provider_whose_key_variable_is_named_but_unset_is_still_a_conflict` |
| G145 | Silencing an alarm is not fixing it. The drift check was returning a false positive on a site that was ahead; passing every non-release ref removed the false positive and the alarm with it, exactly while the site sat on a branch. Narrow the condition, then prove the alarm still fires | `tests/test_workflow_honesty.py::test_the_drift_check_still_alarms_while_the_site_is_built_from_a_branch`, `tests/test_workflow_honesty.py::test_the_secret_that_gates_an_hour_of_work_is_checked_before_the_hour` |
| G146 | A guard added on one input path is not added until every path has it. The `ClusterNetworkPolicy` caveat covered pasted manifests while the live cluster read, which an operator trusts more, still dropped the kind in silence | `tests/test_netpol_cnp_guard.py::test_the_live_cluster_check_reads_clusternetworkpolicies_too`, `tests/test_netpol_cnp_guard.py::test_a_cluster_without_the_current_api_is_not_an_error`, `tests/test_netpol_cnp_guard.py::test_the_read_only_steps_name_the_cluster_s_own_command` |
| G147 | A test that polices a rule is itself a claim, and it is checked by trying to evade it. The missing-secret scanner matched a single exact spelling and missed 13 of 14 equivalents; the address scan read fewer than half the tracked files and skipped the two that ship to PyPI and to the plugin directory | `tests/test_workflow_honesty.py::test_no_job_exits_zero_because_a_secret_it_needs_is_missing`, `tests/test_distribution.py::test_no_real_address_is_published_in_the_repository`, `tests/test_plugin_readme_truth.py::test_the_readme_does_not_claim_every_entry_is_hashed` |
| G149 | A defence that depends on the interpreter raising something is not a defence. `json.loads` was left to stop its own recursion, which held until Python 3.14 stopped applying `sys.getrecursionlimit()` to it: the same nesting bomb then parsed on a CI runner and raised in a smaller container, so no caller could be written against a depth. Bound the input yourself, at a number you choose, before handing it to the thing that recurses | `tests/test_json_depth_guard.py::test_a_ticket_export_nested_past_the_limit_is_refused_rather_than_read_as_empty`, `tests/test_json_depth_guard.py::test_no_module_gains_a_raw_json_or_yaml_loader`, `tests/test_json_depth_guard.py::test_a_scanner_report_nested_past_the_limit_is_refused_as_too_deep` |
| G150 | A sweep is only as wide as its pattern, and the regression test built from the same pattern inherits its blind spot. A `json.loads` sweep keyed on three variable names reached 18 of 71 call sites, and the test left behind could see one. Count the population first, from a pattern that cannot miss, and let the test freeze that count | `tests/test_json_depth_guard.py::test_no_module_gains_a_raw_json_or_yaml_loader`, `tests/test_json_depth_guard.py::test_the_bounded_loaders_are_actually_used_by_the_parsers_that_take_untrusted_input` |
| G151 | "It reads a file" is a claim about the caller, not the function. Four `parse(data)` helpers were given the loose depth ceiling as file readers while their production callers were MCP tool arguments, so a pasted 503-deep document was accepted. Default a limit to the strict case and make the lenient caller ask | `tests/test_json_depth_guard.py::test_a_string_argument_gets_the_tight_ceiling_and_a_file_read_the_loose_one`, `tests/test_json_depth_guard.py::test_the_shared_server_refuses_a_deep_rhacs_policy_export` |
| G152 | Catching the library's error class is not catching the failure. `except yaml.YAMLError` misses `RecursionError` because `yaml.YAMLError` is not a `ValueError`, so a deep document ended the command; and `yaml.CSafeLoader` does not raise at all, it segfaults the process on CPython 3.13. Bound the input before the library touches it | `tests/test_json_depth_guard.py::test_a_deep_exception_list_is_refused_rather_than_crashing_the_tool`, `tests/test_json_depth_guard.py::test_gate_reports_a_deep_manifest_instead_of_crashing`, `tests/test_json_depth_guard.py::test_a_deep_json_array_inside_a_pasted_containerfile_does_not_escape` |
| G148 | A tie broken by the order the input happened to arrive in is a coin toss dressed as a verdict. Two `AdminNetworkPolicy` objects at one priority were decided by document order, so the same two manifests gave `denied` in one file order and `allowed` in the other, while the API defines neither. Where a tier has no determinate answer, say so and propose nothing | `tests/test_netpol_anp.py::test_two_policies_at_one_priority_whose_rules_disagree_are_not_determined`, `tests/test_netpol_anp.py::test_two_policies_at_one_priority_that_agree_still_decide`, `tests/test_netpol_anp.py::test_the_reach_command_prints_and_exits_on_an_undetermined_verdict` |
| G153 | Absence has two causes and a diff must not pick the flattering one. Objects missing from a new snapshot were reported as removed and their findings as fixed, so revoking RBAC on a resource published as a security improvement. Where a reader records what it could not read, that record has to reach the comparison and the rendered output, not just a log line | `tests/test_drift_coverage.py::test_a_finding_on_an_unreadable_object_is_not_reported_as_fixed`, `tests/test_drift_coverage.py::test_a_genuinely_deleted_object_is_still_reported_as_removed`, `tests/test_drift_coverage.py::test_the_report_says_out_loud_that_it_could_not_read_everything` |
| G154 | `all([])` is `True`, so every "are they all acceptable?" check passes an empty collection. An empty corpus shard was marked redistributable and published. Ask whether the collection is non-empty first, in the same expression | `tests/test_corpus_zero_records.py::test_an_empty_shard_is_not_marked_redistributable`, `tests/test_corpus_zero_records.py::test_the_bundle_refuses_zero_records_even_if_the_manifest_claims_otherwise` |
| G155 | Zero is a successful-looking number. A per-source record count of `0` satisfied `isinstance(v, int)` and exited 0, and an `os.replace` of the shard then destroyed the records it was replacing. A result that means the work did not happen must not share a type, or an exit code, with a result that means it did | `tests/test_corpus_zero_records.py::test_a_zero_record_refresh_does_not_destroy_the_shard_it_replaces`, `tests/test_corpus_zero_records.py::test_a_source_that_yields_nothing_makes_corpus_build_exit_nonzero` |
| G156 | `cmd \| tee file` reports `tee`'s exit status. GitHub's default shell is `bash -e` without `pipefail`, so eight steps across the corpus workflows -- including the one that packs the published database bundle -- could not fail when their command did. Set `set -euo pipefail` in any step that pipes | `tests/test_workflow_honesty.py::test_every_piped_step_sets_pipefail` |
| G157 | An empty "what did not run" section is a stronger claim than no section at all: it says nothing was skipped. `gate`'s list covered the two absent-report cases and not the two biggest, so a run that audited no manifests printed "gate passed" above an empty one | `tests/test_false_clean.py::test_the_gate_says_the_manifest_audit_did_not_run_when_it_had_nothing_to_audit`, `tests/test_false_clean.py::test_the_gate_still_lists_nothing_when_everything_ran` |
| G158 | A spec a checker cannot understand must be refused, never scored. A misspelled operator made `_check_value` return `None`, which `_assert` read as "present" and passed -- a free pass on a gating, weight-2.0 assertion, which also disengaged the score clamp | `tests/test_false_clean.py::test_an_assertion_with_no_recognised_operator_is_refused_not_passed`, `tests/test_false_clean.py::test_the_shipped_benchmark_assertions_all_carry_a_recognised_operator` |
| G159 | A redactor must be at least as strong as the detector that gates on it, and the two must share one pattern. The Jira scrubber required a prefix while the privacy audit matched bare ids, so one ticket failed the weekly refresh every week instead of being redacted | `tests/test_false_clean.py::test_the_jira_scrubber_is_at_least_as_strong_as_the_detector_that_gates_it`, `tests/test_false_clean.py::test_the_scrubber_leaves_ordinary_text_alone` |
| G160 | A limit the reader enforces and the writer does not is a limit that fails late. `MAX_MEMBER` lived only in the extractor, so the bundler packed archives nothing could import and reported success. One predicate, one call site | `tests/test_false_clean.py::test_the_bundle_checks_its_own_size_caps_instead_of_leaving_them_to_the_reader` |
| G161 | An empty collection must not be the flattering answer. `all([])` is `True`, `not []` is `True`, and `1.0 if not must else ...` rewards a spec that lists nothing, so three scorers passed a task that asserted nothing. Refuse the empty spec, and sweep the fixtures so the next one is caught when it is written | `tests/test_empty_collection_guards.py::test_a_qa_task_with_nothing_to_mention_says_it_checked_no_claims`, `tests/test_empty_collection_guards.py::test_an_evaluation_with_no_outcomes_is_not_passed`, `tests/test_empty_collection_guards.py::test_no_shipped_fixture_asserts_nothing` |
| G162 | A guard in the writer does not protect the reader. The check against an empty corpus went into `db_bundle.build`, so kube-noob could not publish one -- but `db-check`, which is what a user runs on a bundle they downloaded, still reported clean and exited 0 on it. Put the check where the claim is made | `tests/test_empty_collection_guards.py::test_db_check_refuses_a_bundle_manifest_that_declares_no_shards` |
| G163 | "ready" is a stronger word than "no findings". `run_protocol` set `status = "ready" if not blockers else "blocked"`, so a run with no documents -- where no rule ran at all -- reported ready. A verdict word must be earned by something having been evaluated, not by an absence | `tests/test_empty_collection_guards.py::test_the_protocol_does_not_call_a_run_ready_when_it_audited_nothing` |
| G164 | A test scoped to the file where a wrong claim was found does not stop the claim living elsewhere. G136 removed "rebuilt daily" from `docs/use-cases.md` and the test it added read only that file, so the same sentence stayed in the chart's `values.yaml` and the site kept republishing it. Scope the sweep to every tracked file and allowlist the exceptions by name | `tests/test_distribution.py::test_nothing_promises_an_artefact_that_has_never_published` |
| G165 | An MCP result is spent out of the caller's context window, so bytes that carry nothing are a cost like any other. `audit_manifests` repeated each rule's `title`, `severity`, `stage`, `category`, `remediation`, `citations`, `stackroxPolicies` and `controls` on every finding it produced -- 105 findings over 19 rules on `examples/`, so each constant appeared about five times and 54% of the result was copies. Hoist the per-rule constants into one `ruleInfo` map keyed by rule id and keep an exact inverse, so the saving is a hoist rather than a guess about what the caller does not need | `tests/test_compact_output.py::test_compacting_is_lossless` |
| G166 | A flag that changes the emitted shape must not change the verdict a gate reads. Compaction moves `severity` off the findings, so an exit code computed from the emitted report instead of the audit would have turned every failing `audit --compact` green. Compact the copy that is printed; compute the gate from the report | `tests/test_compact_output.py::test_audit_compact_on_the_cli_changes_the_shape_but_not_the_verdict` |
| G167 | Writing a result to the file a caller named and also inlining it spends the context twice, and leaves the result *larger* with `-o` than without it. `policy generate -o FILE` returned the whole 358 KB rendered policy set in the JSON as well as writing it, 385 965 bytes against 385 866. A caller that names a file has said where it wants the bytes: send the path and the size instead | `tests/test_compact_output.py::test_asking_for_a_file_does_not_also_inline_the_whole_policy_set` |
| G168 | Filtering the evidence before the summary is computed deletes the summary. `Toolkit.audit` set `r.findings = kept` and then called `as_dict()`, which builds `controlSummary` from that same list -- so excepting a finding removed its control from the compliance record, and a control somebody had signed off on read exactly like one nothing had evaluated. An accepted exception is evidence about a control, not the absence of it: keep the control named and count the excepted evidence apart | `tests/test_exception_control_evidence.py::test_an_exception_keeps_the_control_in_the_summary` |
| G169 | A saving applied to one of two lists is not a saving. `compact_report` hoisted the per-rule constants out of `findings` and left the `excepted` list alone, so every caller with an exceptions file -- which is every CI caller -- got hoisted findings beside un-hoisted ones in the same report, and `expand_report` restored only half of it. The round-trip test missed it because its fixture called `analyze(...).as_dict()` directly and so never had an `excepted` key: test the shape through the entry point a caller actually uses | `tests/test_compact_output.py::test_the_excepted_findings_are_compacted_too` |
| G170 | Do not report the size of what you rendered as the size of what you wrote. `policy generate -o` returned `len(text.encode("utf-8"))` while writing with `write_text`, which translates newlines on Windows, so the count was wrong on one platform only and no Linux test could see it. Write the bytes you counted | `tests/test_compact_output.py::test_asking_for_a_file_does_not_also_inline_the_whole_policy_set` |
| G171 | A table with a reading date answers questions past that date as if it had read them. `REMOVED_APIS` was read at Kubernetes 1.37, and for a 1.38 target `api_history` returned `removedInTarget: []` and `validate` produced no removed-API finding -- the same answer a genuinely clean version gets. Data has a ceiling; name it in the data (`CHECKED_THROUGH`) and carry it into any answer past it, rather than leaving the caller to infer coverage from an empty list | `tests/test_version_coverage.py::test_a_report_targeting_an_unchecked_version_says_what_it_did_not_check` |
| G172 | Disclosing what you could not read does not excuse a verdict that ignores it. `policy reach` named `ClusterNetworkPolicy` under `notEvaluated` and caveated the proposed fix, then returned `verdict: allowed` and exit 0 -- so a CI gate merged on an answer a cluster-scoped `Deny` reverses, and the disclosure sat in a list no pipeline reads. When something you cannot evaluate outranks what you can, the verdict is unknown, not the answer your partial view produced | `tests/test_cluster_scoped_undetermined.py::test_the_cli_exit_code_stops_a_gate_instead_of_passing_it` |
| G173 | Fixing a size problem on the surface that has an escape hatch leaves the one that does not. `policy generate -o FILE` stopped inlining 358 KB of YAML, but `PolicyGenerateIn` has no `output` field and an MCP caller has no filesystem, so the tool still returned 380,670 bytes -- about 95,000 tokens, more than most callers hold. | G174 | A test written against an invented input passes and proves nothing. | G193 | Absence read as a DEFECT is the same error as absence read as a pass, and the DRA work ran into both sides within one section. KN-C015 reports a Pod naming a `ResourceClaim` or `ResourceClaimTemplate` no document defines in the Pod's namespace, which is a real post-deploy Pending; it is CROSS_OBJECT and returns early on `partial_bundle`, because a claim in a file you did not pass is not a missing claim. The reference is namespaced and kind-exact: a `ResourceClaim` named `gpu-pool` does not satisfy a `resourceClaimTemplateName: gpu-pool`. The matching `deviceClassName` rule was designed and then DELETED: a `DeviceClass` is cluster-scoped and shipped by the driver, so it is absent from almost every bundle and the rule would have fired on every correct repository. KN-ST001 gets away with the same shape only because `KNOWN_STORAGE_CLASSES` exists; with no catalogue of device classes there is nothing to decide against. KN-S031 reports `adminAccess: true`, which upstream documents as ignoring every ordinary claim to the device with respect to access modes and allocations -- so it reaches devices another tenant holds exclusively, and the only gate is a namespace label. When the Namespace IS in the bundle and lacks `resource.kubernetes.io/admin-access: "true"` the message says the API server will refuse the object, because then it is a hard verdict rather than a risk; absent the Namespace it stays the privilege finding. `adminAccess` is read at exactly one path, `requests[].exactly.adminAccess`: `DeviceSubRequest` under `firstAvailable` omits the field on purpose, so a key of that name there is not the API field and means nothing. Every semantic here came from upstream's own types and validation, never from documentation prose, because the bundled schemas ship with descriptions stripped | `tests/test_dra_claims.py::test_a_claim_in_the_bundle_clears_the_reference_and_an_absent_one_does_not`, `tests/test_dra_claims.py::test_the_reference_is_namespaced`, `tests/test_dra_claims.py::test_a_template_reference_needs_a_template_not_a_claim`, `tests/test_dra_claims.py::test_a_partial_bundle_cannot_tell_an_absent_claim_from_one_you_did_not_pass`, `tests/test_dra_claims.py::test_admin_access_is_reported_on_a_claim_and_on_a_template`, `tests/test_dra_claims.py::test_admin_access_false_is_not_a_finding`, `tests/test_dra_claims.py::test_a_namespace_in_the_bundle_without_the_label_makes_it_a_refusal_not_a_warning`, `tests/test_dra_claims.py::test_admin_access_is_not_read_from_a_firstavailable_subrequest` |
| G194 | A manifest that states a digest has to describe an archive whose digest is a property of its CONTENT, not of the machine that packed it. `scripts/krew_manifest.py` fixes the member name, mode, mtime, uid, gid and user name, and gzips with `mtime=0`; without that every build of identical content produced a different hash and `--check` could never pass, so the manifest would have to be hand-edited each release -- which is how a published asset and the digest that is supposed to verify it drift apart. The entry-point script is a PASSTHROUGH and the verb table was deleted rather than written: a mapping from kubectl-shaped verbs onto CLI subcommands is a second copy of the command set, and it goes stale the first time one moves. The one real bug here was found only by running the failing path: the "nothing installed" message used a heredoc fed to `cat`, and that message is reached exactly when `PATH` may be unusable, so it printed `cat: not found` instead of the install instructions. | G195 | A red team on the four DRA rules found five wrong answers, and four of them share one root: a rule that reads ONE shape of a thing, or trusts a value it did not check. **The API version changes the shape.** `resource.k8s.io/v1beta1` FLATTENS a device request -- `adminAccess` sits on the request where v1 and v1beta2 put it under `exactly` -- and the bundled schemas still serve v1beta1, so KN-S031 was silent on every v1beta1 claim. Upstream normalises between the two shapes for exactly this reason. The gate works in both directions: on v1 the request has no `adminAccess` property at all, so a key of that name there is a schema error for KN-V007, not this field. **Presence is not truthiness.** Upstream tests whether a pointer is set, and `resourceClaimName: ""` decodes to a non-nil `*string`, so it really is "both sources named"; reading it as falsy let that case through and also mis-diagnosed `""` alone as a missing source when it is an invalid name. **One rule owns one defect.** KN-C015 said "the Pod is admitted and then stays Pending" about objects the API server refuses outright -- an entry naming two sources, or a duplicate claim name -- contradicting KN-V021 in the same report. It now skips what KN-V021 owns, and KN-V021 reports the duplicate that nothing reported before. **A name two siblings share is not a key.** `locator()` keyed list items on `name`, so two different defects on two entries called `gpu` hashed to ONE fingerprint and a baseline entry for either suppressed the other; it now falls back to the index when the name is ambiguous, while a unique sibling keeps its name (G190 said items with no natural key keep the index -- a repeated name is no key). **A CROSS_OBJECT rule needs the context cache to fetch its kind.** KN-C015 was the only member whose kind the webhook never fetches, so with `admission.lookups` on it warned that a ResourceClaim was missing from a bundle it never looked in, on every correct DRA Pod. KN-V022 reports it. The condition is PRESENCE of a non-null value, not a non-empty list, because upstream tests `spec.Resources.Claims != nil`: `claims: []` is set and refused, `claims: null` is not set. This was surfaced by the DRA red team as an uncovered case and explicitly NOT treated as a KN-V020 miss -- KN-V020 reads containers only, which is right, because the supported place for a claim reference is a container | `tests/test_dra_claims.py::test_pod_level_resource_claims_are_forbidden_outright`, `tests/test_dra_claims.py::test_an_empty_pod_level_claims_list_is_still_forbidden`, `tests/test_dra_claims.py::test_pod_level_claims_are_found_in_a_nested_template`, `tests/test_dra_claims.py::test_pod_level_resources_without_claims_is_not_a_finding` |
| G197 | A compliance format is a contract, and three of its rules are not guessable -- each was read off NIST's published schema and catalogue, and each would have produced a document that looks right and is wrong. **A finding may not target a control:** `finding-target.type` is an enum of exactly `statement-id` and `objective-id`, so an OSCAL finding targets the control's objective id, `ac-6.10_obj`, which the catalogue defines. **Control ids are catalogue ids, not the labels:** `AC-6(10)` becomes `ac-6.10`, and parentheses are not legal in OSCAL's `TokenDatatype` at all, so emitting the label yields a document that cannot validate. All 28 of kube-noob's controls and their `_obj` ids were confirmed present in `NIST_SP-800-53_rev5_catalog.json` (catalogue 5.2.0), and the mapping is pinned in a fixture because the catalogue is 10 MB. **Validation is ajv, not Python's `jsonschema`:** `TokenDatatype` is `^(\p{L}|_)(\p{L}|\p{N}|[.\-_])*$` and Python's `re` cannot compile a Unicode property escape, so `jsonschema` raises `PatternError` the moment validation reaches a token -- including on a document whose only fault is an illegal control id. (This entry first said it SKIPS the pattern and would call an invalid document valid. A red team disproved that: it fails loudly, not quietly. The conclusion was right for the wrong reason, and `test_python_jsonschema_cannot_validate_this_schema_at_all` now pins the real behaviour so the claim cannot drift back.) CI installs ajv and then asserts the test did not SKIP, because a skipped check is the same absence-read-as-pass this project keeps fixing. The honesty rules carry over from G191: `satisfied` is emitted only where the coverage record says that control's rules ran and found nothing, a control nothing evaluated is excluded rather than passed, with no coverage record nothing is satisfied at all, an exception is `not-satisfied` with reason `other` rather than a pass, and the required `import-ap` points at a fragment whose remarks say no plan was imported rather than naming one that does not exist | `tests/test_oscal.py::test_a_finding_targets_an_objective_because_the_schema_forbids_a_control`, `tests/test_oscal.py::test_every_control_resolves_to_the_pinned_nist_catalogue_id`, `tests/test_oscal.py::test_no_emitted_id_would_break_the_token_pattern`, `tests/test_oscal.py::test_python_jsonschema_cannot_validate_this_schema_at_all`, `tests/test_oscal.py::test_the_document_validates_against_nists_published_schema`, `tests/test_oscal.py::test_a_control_no_rule_evaluated_is_excluded_and_never_satisfied`, `tests/test_oscal.py::test_without_a_coverage_record_nothing_is_reported_satisfied`, `tests/test_oscal.py::test_the_missing_assessment_plan_is_stated_rather_than_faked`, `tests/test_oscal.py::test_the_document_is_reproducible_byte_for_byte` |
| G198 | The same absent field means different things on different platforms, and a rule that ignores that reports a platform's design as a defect. Pod Security admission is configured by namespace labels (`pod-security.kubernetes.io/enforce`, upstream's own constant), and the obvious rule -- "flag a namespace with no enforce label" -- is WRONG on OpenShift: the label syncer sets `warn` and `audit` from the SCCs a namespace's service accounts can use and deliberately not `enforce`, because OpenShift enforces the privileged profile globally and leaves real enforcement to SCCs. So KN-S032 is silent on the OpenShift family for an absent label. It reports an explicit `enforce: privileged` on every platform, because that is a decision rather than a default. And on vanilla Kubernetes it does NOT say the namespace enforces nothing: the level is whatever the cluster's PodSecurity AdmissionConfiguration default is, and no manifest shows that, so the finding says the answer is not in the repository -- the same three-valued discipline the policy commands use. `baseline` and `restricted` are judgements about the workload, not defects, so neither is reported. | G199 | A rule that FIRED must never be counted clean because the caller filtered its finding out of this view. `--min-severity high` filters `report.findings` but not the record of which rules ran, and `controlSummary` was built from the filtered findings plus the unfiltered evaluated set -- so every rule whose finding was withheld fell into `clean`. CP-10 went from "3 findings, 6 clean" to "0 findings, 9 clean", and the OSCAL document then reported all 28 controls `satisfied` for a bundle with a privileged container, a root user and a plaintext password, at exit 0. This is the same absence-read-as-pass as G191, reintroduced by a flag: G191 fixed "nothing ran" looking like "nothing found", and the filter recreated it as "found but hidden" looking like "nothing found". The report now carries `fired_rules`, recorded BEFORE the filter, and those rules are counted as `withheld` -- not a finding in this view, and emphatically not clean. The invariant the tests hold is that `clean` does not depend on `--min-severity` at all, that findings + excepted + clean + withheld + notEvaluated accounts for every rule of a control at every filter level, and that the OSCAL verdict per control is identical at every filter level. A `withheld` control is `not-satisfied` with reason `other`, never a pass. The exceptions rebuild carries the fired set too, or filing one exception would reopen the hole | `tests/test_coverage_min_severity.py::test_a_withheld_finding_does_not_become_clean`, `tests/test_coverage_min_severity.py::test_no_control_is_clean_only_because_of_the_filter`, `tests/test_coverage_min_severity.py::test_the_buckets_always_account_for_every_rule_of_a_control`, `tests/test_coverage_min_severity.py::test_oscal_satisfies_a_control_only_when_nothing_was_found_or_withheld`, `tests/test_coverage_min_severity.py::test_the_oscal_verdict_no_longer_depends_on_the_severity_filter`, `tests/test_coverage_min_severity.py::test_the_cli_oscal_document_cannot_be_laundered_by_min_severity` |
| G200 | A red team on the OSCAL emitter found five more defects, and they fall into two shapes. **An identity must cover everything that changes what it identifies.** The uuid was keyed on platform, version and resource names, so a default run and a `--min-severity critical` run -- one reporting fifteen controls not-satisfied, the other none -- produced the same `assessment-results` uuid, the same `result` uuid, and fifteen `finding` uuids that flipped verdict without changing identity. The key now includes every finding's fingerprint, every control's buckets and the imported plan. Relatedly the observation list was ordered by `(severity, docIndex, line)`, so listing the same two files in the other order gave the same uuids and different bytes; it is sorted by uuid now. And an observation uuid keyed on the finding fingerprint alone collided for two containers failing one pod-level check -- the fingerprint excludes the message by design (G190), which is right for a finding's cross-run identity and wrong for "a thing that was seen", so the message joins the observation key and a consumer resolving `related-observations` can reach both. **Anything a manifest supplies can violate a datatype.** `property.value` is `StringDatatype` (`^\S(.*\S)?$`), and a YAML folded scalar yields `Deployment/shop/orders-api\n` while the typo KN-V007 exists to report yields the path `spec.bad field ` -- both produced documents no OSCAL tool will ingest, at exit 1, so a pipeline read it as business as usual. Prop values now collapse whitespace and drop if empty. `--oscal-import-ap` was passed through verbatim, so `ap plan.json` or a Windows path produced an invalid `uri-reference`; it is checked before a document is built around it. Two honesty gaps closed with them: `--skip-unreadable` promises the skipped files are listed "so a gate never reads a partial audit as a clean one", and with `--oscal` the document IS the report, so the skipped files are named in its remarks and still printed on stderr; and `include-controls`, `exclude-controls` and whether a finding is emitted now read ONE predicate, because they disagreed on excepted controls and could name a control under `exclude-controls` while giving it a finding | `tests/test_oscal.py::test_the_uuid_changes_when_the_verdict_changes`, `tests/test_oscal.py::test_the_import_ap_is_part_of_the_identity`, `tests/test_oscal.py::test_the_document_does_not_depend_on_the_order_the_documents_arrived_in`, `tests/test_oscal.py::test_two_containers_failing_one_pod_level_check_get_two_observation_uuids`, `tests/test_oscal.py::test_a_name_with_whitespace_does_not_break_the_property_pattern`, `tests/test_oscal.py::test_a_field_path_with_a_trailing_space_does_not_break_it_either`, `tests/test_oscal.py::test_an_import_ap_that_is_not_a_uri_reference_is_refused`, `tests/test_oscal.py::test_skipped_files_are_named_in_the_document_not_only_on_stderr`, `tests/test_oscal.py::test_a_control_is_never_both_given_a_finding_and_excluded`, `tests/test_oscal.py::test_a_check_that_maps_to_no_control_is_declared_rather_than_silently_dropped` |
| G201 | A command that already computed the answer must publish it, or a second command built on its output has to guess. `gate` runs `analyze` and therefore HAD the coverage record all along; it published `findings` and not `controlSummary`, so `gate --oscal` could only ever report controls as not-satisfied and never as checked-and-clean -- a weaker document than the same input gives through `audit`. The gate report now carries `controlSummary`, `resources` and `kubernetesVersion`, built from the same `controls.summary` call with the gate's own kept and excepted rule ids. The test that matters is not that the flag works but that the two commands AGREE bucket for bucket, and it compares them on a directory: `gate -f rendered.yaml` is documented as the whole rendered namespace and treats it as complete, while `audit -f one-file.yaml` is a partial bundle by design (G186), so comparing those two is comparing different questions. | G202 | Two correct fixes can combine into a wrong answer. G186 made the rules that read other objects SILENT on a partial bundle, because they cannot tell a real gap from a file you did not pass. G191 then started reporting a control whose rules ran and found nothing as `clean`. `run_rules` appended a rule id BEFORE calling it, so a rule the guard skipped produced no findings and was recorded as evaluated -- and `audit -f one-file.yaml` reported SC-7 as "0 findings, 12 clean" when two of those twelve rules had declined to look. Neither fix was wrong on its own, which is why nothing caught it: the bug lives in the seam. The id is now appended AFTER the check and only when the rule did not call `AuditContext.cannot_decide`, which the four fully-guarded rules do (KN-N001, KN-N013, KN-C008, KN-C015). A rule whose guard skips one branch while evaluating the rest -- KN-ST001's undeclared-StorageClass branch -- does NOT register, because it did run. | G203 | The default of an absent field decides whether its absence is a finding, and it is not always the safe one. OLM's `Subscription.GetInstallPlanApproval` returns `Manual` only when the field SAYS Manual and `Automatic` otherwise, so an absent `installPlanApproval` chooses automatic upgrades: KN-S034 reports the absent field, and its message says so, because a reader who assumes absence defers the decision has it backwards. The enum is case-sensitive, so `manual` is reported too rather than read as Manual -- the same stance KN-S030 takes on an invalid `failurePolicy`. The other two in this set are straightforward by comparison but were written from the upstream type rather than prose: `MachineConfigPool.spec.paused` is documented as stopping the rendered config AND the machine update, which is why KN-S033 names OS, kubelet and runtime rather than just "updates", and it fires only on `is True` so `paused: false`, an absent field and the string `'true'` are all silent. KN-C016 is absence-based and cross-object, so it registers `cannot_decide` on a partial bundle (G202) and joins CROSS_OBJECT; it is LOW and says in the finding that a ClusterResourceQuota or a cluster default may cover it, because the bundle cannot show that -- a HIGH here would be a verdict the input does not support | `tests/test_underserved_rules.py::test_an_absent_approval_field_is_reported_because_absent_means_automatic`, `tests/test_underserved_rules.py::test_an_unknown_approval_value_is_reported_rather_than_assumed_safe`, `tests/test_underserved_rules.py::test_a_subscription_from_another_api_group_is_not_an_olm_subscription`, `tests/test_underserved_rules.py::test_a_paused_pool_says_what_stops`, `tests/test_underserved_rules.py::test_an_absent_paused_field_is_silent`, `tests/test_underserved_rules.py::test_the_quota_rule_declines_to_decide_on_a_partial_bundle`, `tests/test_underserved_rules.py::test_only_the_missing_one_is_named`, `tests/test_underserved_rules.py::test_one_finding_per_namespace_not_per_workload` |
| G204 | A red team on the four newest rules found six wrong answers, and the worst is the shape to remember: **a rule that checks a value must treat an unrecognised value as a defect, not as a judgement.** KN-S032 reported `enforce: privileged` and cleared everything else, so `Privileged` with a capital P was SILENT while an absent label was MEDIUM -- the rule was quieter about a namespace that looks privileged than about one with no label at all, which is a typo in the very label it exists to check buying a pass. `PSA_LEVELS` was defined and asserted against upstream and then never read; the rule now reports any value outside those three lower-case strings, quotes it, and does NOT claim what the cluster does with an invalid value, because that was not verified. **A platform-specific exemption must be gated on the platform.** `SYSTEM_NAMESPACES` mixes `openshift` with the `kube-*` ones, so `openshift-apps` was skipped on vanilla Kubernetes where no syncer owns it; `platform_owned_namespace` now splits them, and the old test that pinned this ran only on the default platform. KN-C016 took four fixes, three of them the same error in different clothes -- a message that states something its own input contradicts. A `ClusterResourceQuota` in the bundle is exactly what the remediation tells the reader to confirm, and the finding said "this bundle cannot show that"; an unnamespaced ResourceQuota lands wherever it is applied, so a bundle carrying one is ambiguous rather than missing it (the kustomize and `apply -n` shape); and the platform's own namespaces were being told to add quotas. The fourth: the kind filter was the long-running kinds plus `Pod`, so a bare one-shot Pod was reported and a recurring CronJob was not, which is backwards -- a runaway CronJob is the textbook reason for `count/jobs.batch`. KN-S033 now checks its API group, because KN-S034 already guarded that and the inconsistency was the only argument needed | `tests/test_pod_security.py::test_a_value_that_is_not_one_of_the_three_levels_is_reported`, `tests/test_pod_security.py::test_an_openshift_namespace_name_is_only_special_on_openshift`, `tests/test_pod_security.py::test_the_same_namespace_declared_twice_is_reported_once`, `tests/test_pod_security.py::test_a_namespace_with_no_usable_name_is_left_to_the_schema_rule`, `tests/test_underserved_rules.py::test_a_cronjob_namespace_is_reported_like_any_other_workload`, `tests/test_underserved_rules.py::test_a_cluster_resource_quota_in_the_bundle_clears_the_quota_half`, `tests/test_underserved_rules.py::test_an_unnamespaced_quota_is_not_called_absent`, `tests/test_underserved_rules.py::test_the_platform_owns_its_own_namespaces_quotas`, `tests/test_underserved_rules.py::test_the_pool_rule_checks_its_api_group_like_the_subscription_rule_does` |
| G205 | When a rule you added reports your OWN artifact, the finding is the first evidence that the rule works -- and weakening it so your artifact passes is the failure this project keeps correcting in other people's reasoning. KN-S030 reports `failurePolicy: Ignore`, and kube-noob's own admission chart sets it deliberately: the webhook turns findings into kubectl warnings and denies only the rules listed in `admission.enforce`, so an audit webhook that is down must not stop a cluster admitting objects. Both are right. Lowering the severity was considered and refused -- MEDIUM would still fail the gate and LOW would understate a silently skipped policy gate for every other user -- so the decision is recorded in `.github/kube-noob-exceptions.yaml` with an owner, a reason and an expiry, which is what the exceptions feature exists for and means the repository now dogfoods it. The process lesson is separate and sharper, and it cost three CI rounds. **A test whose expected value and whose subject both come from the same assumption proves only that the assumption is self-consistent.** The first version of this exception named the webhook `kn-kube-noob-admission`, inferred from the `kube-noob.admissionName` helper -- which names the SERVICE. The real object is `{{ $name }}.{{ $ns }}.kube-noob.io`, declared on the line above the `webhooks:` block that was read. The test then hardcoded that same invented name, matched it against itself, and passed locally while CI failed with `unused EXC-ADM-001: covers no finding`. It now DERIVES the name from the template's name expression, the `admissionName` helper and the release and namespace the workflow renders with, so a change to any of the three fails here rather than in a workflow -- and the derivation independently reproduced the string CI printed, which is the cross-check the first version had no way to perform. Two smaller process errors sit underneath it: the chart's DEFAULT values do not render the webhook, so reading the defaults said the rule could not fire while a later step sets `admission.enabled=true`; and `main`'s own CI status should have been checked BEFORE offering a pre-existing-failure hypothesis, not after it was disproved | `tests/test_rules_dns_and_webhook.py::test_a_webhook_that_fails_open_is_reported`, `tests/test_rules_dns_and_webhook.py::test_our_own_exceptions_file_still_covers_our_own_webhook` |
| G206 | A compliance record kept by hand drifts from the thing it records, and the drift is invisible because the guard was written in one direction. `docs/compliance.md` is the document a reader checks kube-noob's claims against, and it was wrong three ways at once: the heading said "The 78 audit rules" with 89 in the code; SEVEN controls that rules map to had no title row -- every parenthesised enhancement plus `SC-4` -- so a reader who saw `IA-5(7)` on a rule had nowhere to look it up; and the only guard asserted that every rule HAS a row, never that every row still has a rule, so a deleted or renamed rule would have left its row standing forever. The tables are generated now, from `list_rules()` and `RULE_CONTROLS`/`TITLES`, and `--check` runs in `scripts/check.sh` and in CI beside the other generators. Three things the generator had to get right. **Generating from `RULE_CONTROLS` alone deletes what the document evidences another way:** `SC-13` is cited by `kube-noob doctor` and the FIPS guidance and maps to no rule, so it lives in `POSTURE_CONTROLS` and a test pins that it survives. **A NIST enhancement title contains a literal `\|`** (`Least Privilege \| Privileged Accounts`), which written raw into a Markdown cell splits the row into three columns -- which is exactly how the committed table rendered -- so cells are escaped on the way out and unescaped on the way back, and what a test compares against `TITLES` is the real title. **A regex rewrite of a region must be checked against the whole document, not against itself:** a blank line inside the table splits it, the pattern then matched only the first half, and the first version regenerated that half and left 49 orphaned rows below it -- a document listing 49 rules twice, written at exit 0. That is the same absence-read-as-pass shape the rest of this tool exists to catch. It now counts rule rows across the document and refuses when any sit outside the table, and refuses when rows follow the titles table, rather than half-rewriting | `tests/test_compliance_doc.py::test_the_committed_document_is_current`, `tests/test_compliance_doc.py::test_every_rule_has_exactly_one_row_and_every_row_has_a_rule`, `tests/test_compliance_doc.py::test_every_control_a_rule_maps_to_has_a_title_row`, `tests/test_compliance_doc.py::test_a_posture_control_is_kept_even_though_no_rule_maps_to_it`, `tests/test_compliance_doc.py::test_a_damaged_table_fails_loudly_rather_than_being_rewritten_twice`, `tests/test_compliance_doc.py::test_a_renamed_heading_refuses_instead_of_reporting_the_document_current` |
| G207 | **Evaluating a policy the cluster's CNI ignores is not a cautious answer, it is a wrong one.** `policy reach` evaluates AdminNetworkPolicy and BaselineAdminNetworkPolicy with OVN-Kubernetes semantics, and Calico removed support for both in 3.32 in favour of `ClusterNetworkPolicy`: "Calico does not enforce them from 3.32 onward, so they stop taking effect silently" (Tigera upgrade notes, retrieved 2026-11-05). On such a cluster the tier kube-noob trusted FIRST is the one doing nothing, so a `Deny` it reported was a deny that was not happening and an `Allow` was permission nobody had granted -- and both read as a confident verdict. `--cni NAME[:VERSION]` fixes it with three answers rather than two, because the two-valued version is where the trap is. `enforced` (the upstream network-policy-api implementations page lists OVN-Kubernetes, Antrea, Kube-OVN, kube-network-policies and Calico 3.29-3.31) behaves as before. `not-enforced` drops the tier, lets the NetworkPolicies answer -- which can be the OPPOSITE verdict -- and reports each policy as `inertPolicies` with the reason and the replacement kind, printed on the line under the verdict because a finding only the JSON carries is one the operator does not read. `unknown` (Cilium, which that page lists with a tracking issue and no stated support) drops the tier AND makes the verdict `undetermined`, because evaluating it assumes enforcement and ignoring it assumes the reverse; it does not borrow the `tier_unreadable` wording, since the policy was read and the doubt is about the CNI. Three constraints the design turns on: a CNI absent from the table is `unknown`, never enforced, so the table never guesses in its owner's favour; Calico with no version is `unknown` too, because for that CNI the version is the whole answer; and an unknown CNI with nothing in that tier answers normally, since the doubt is about the tier and not about the cluster. The default, with no flag, is unchanged -- an opt-in, so no existing answer moves under anyone. Not built in `policy migrate-check` as the roadmap guessed: that command is a Kyverno rule-by-rule lint, and the wrong answer was coming out of `policy reach` | `tests/test_cni_admin_tier.py::test_the_table_answers_only_where_upstream_does`, `tests/test_cni_admin_tier.py::test_every_entry_names_where_it_comes_from`, `tests/test_cni_admin_tier.py::test_an_admin_deny_a_cni_ignores_does_not_deny`, `tests/test_cni_admin_tier.py::test_an_admin_allow_a_cni_ignores_does_not_allow`, `tests/test_cni_admin_tier.py::test_a_baseline_a_cni_ignores_leaves_the_traffic_allowed`, `tests/test_cni_admin_tier.py::test_an_unknown_cni_makes_the_answer_undetermined_rather_than_either`, `tests/test_cni_admin_tier.py::test_an_unknown_cni_with_no_admin_policy_answers_normally`, `tests/test_cni_admin_tier.py::test_no_cni_keeps_the_documented_ovn_kubernetes_semantics`, `tests/test_cni_admin_tier.py::test_the_flag_reaches_the_evaluator_and_the_terminal`, `tests/test_cni_admin_tier.py::test_a_bad_flag_value_is_refused_by_the_schema` |
| G208 | **A rule can only be as strong as the thing its input proves, and the numeric case proves less than the named one.** KN-V012 reports a NAMED `targetPort` no selected container declares, at HIGH, because a name must resolve to a declared port or the Service gets no endpoints at all. KN-V023 reports the numeric case and cannot borrow any of that: `containerPort` is informational, so a Service pointing at 8080 works whenever a container listens on 8080, declared or not. A rule that reported every undeclared numeric port would fire on correct repositories -- the absence-read-as-DEFECT failure, which is the one this project keeps having to resist while hunting the absence-read-as-pass one. So the rule narrows to the case where there IS evidence: these containers declare ports, and the Service points outside that set, so one of the two is wrong. Where the containers declare no ports, there is no signal and the rule is silent; it is LOW; and the finding says Kubernetes does not require the declaration, because a LOW that reads like a broken Service sends someone to fix working YAML. Two details carry the same stance: an omitted `targetPort` IS checked, because it defaults to `port` -- treating its absence as safe would be the original defect in the other direction -- and a port declared only on an init container does not clear a target, because a Service never reaches one. A workload in another namespace does not clear it either | `tests/test_numeric_target_port.py::test_a_numeric_target_outside_the_declared_ports_is_reported`, `tests/test_numeric_target_port.py::test_the_message_says_it_is_not_proof`, `tests/test_numeric_target_port.py::test_a_workload_that_declares_no_ports_at_all_is_silent`, `tests/test_numeric_target_port.py::test_a_named_target_is_left_to_the_rule_that_owns_it`, `tests/test_numeric_target_port.py::test_an_omitted_target_port_defaults_to_the_service_port_and_is_checked`, `tests/test_numeric_target_port.py::test_an_init_container_port_does_not_satisfy_a_service_target`, `tests/test_numeric_target_port.py::test_a_workload_in_another_namespace_does_not_satisfy_the_target`, `tests/test_numeric_target_port.py::test_a_service_whose_workload_is_not_in_the_bundle_is_silent` |
| G209 | A red team on `--cni` (G207) found nine wrong answers, and the two worst are both the house defect wearing the new feature's clothes. **`calico:3` was read as ENFORCED.** The comparison truncated the TABLE's bound to the length of the INPUT, so `(3,) >= (3,)` answered yes, and `3` does not distinguish 3.31 from 3.32 -- an unknown read as a pass inside the function written to stop exactly that. A version with fewer components than the bound is now `unknown`, like `calico` with no version. Past the removal the answer stays `not-enforced` however far past, because Tigera's wording is "from 3.32 onward"; a major-only `4` is `unknown` for the same precision reason. **An `unknown` CNI withheld answers that were the same either way.** Dropping the tier guesses; evaluating it guesses; so the first version refused to answer whenever any admin policy existed -- which on a Cilium cluster made `policy reach` unable to answer ANY question and fail CI for nothing. Three-valued does not mean discarding an invariant answer: both readings are evaluated now and the verdict is withheld only where they disagree, with the withheld reading's explanation reported because it is the one that holds either way. **A direction never evaluated was summarised as an answer:** `decided` came from the evaluated directions only, so one allowed direction beside a hostNetwork direction read as "the NetworkPolicies would allow this traffic". An existing test had pinned that wording; it now asserts the direction's own reason instead, and the fix lands on the older unevaluated-policy branch too. **A 403 on a tier the caller just called inert withheld the verdict:** `_apply_cni` returns early on an empty tier and `tier_unreadable` was set afterwards, so the flag's whole promise was unapplied on the one path where the operator cannot read the policies for themselves -- the namespace-scoped `--live` case. That tier is now read and only the FAILURE of the read is dismissed -- the first version of this fix stopped reading the tier altogether, which dismissed the 403 and lost the inert finding with it (reversed in G213). The rest: a withheld tier is the case where a proposed NetworkPolicy is MOST likely a no-op and was the only branch with no caveat; `readOnlyNextSteps` sent the reader to the dead kind and never named `ClusterNetworkPolicy`; `kube-network-policies` sat in the enforcing set on a claim its own source does not make (upstream links a conformance report and states no support level, so it is `unknown`); an unparseable `cni` was only rejected when the tier was non-empty; and a list took a singular verb. One finding was NOT acted on: `policy reach` exits 0 when an inert policy flips a denial to an allow.
