Proven: with the `<=` form restored, `tests/test_distribution.py` passes 23 of 23 against a formula serving 0.9.2 in a 0.10.0 tree. An update that never happened read as a pass, in the one output path this project does not control end to end. The assertion is equality now, so `main` is red for as long as its formula serves a version this repository is not, and the message names both versions and the command. The job commits to `chore/brew-formula-<version>` -- a branch, which protection allows -- and then EXITS 1 with the command to land it, because a branch nobody merges is an update that did not happen and a job reporting success over it would be the same defect one level up. It cannot be moved before the tag: the formula pins PyPI-assigned URL paths, which do not exist until the upload, so `needs: publish-pypi` and the ten-attempt wait stay. The one quiet success is preserved: a re-run whose formula is already current is still a no-op. The rule for the next writer: a distribution channel you cannot write to automatically is not a channel you may assume followed -- assert the version it serves, and let the gate that owns the repository say when it has not One of this change's own tests then asserted the formula's host with `"files.pythonhosted.org" in text`, and CodeQL raised it as a high-severity "incomplete URL substring sanitization": that substring also matches `https://elsewhere.example/files.pythonhosted.org/...`. | G361 | **Nothing checked whether a release was waiting to be cut, and the website is built from the latest release.** kube-noob.com takes its version, rule counts and install commands from the newest GitHub release; the site repository rebuilds every six hours and skips when nothing changed, so rebuild frequency was never the thing -- a week with no release is a week of the previous release's facts, however busy `main` has been. G358 was that at its worst: the site could not build at all for six days and `site-sync` reported success throughout, because the published site and the latest release agreed while nothing moved. The release cadence was the one link in the chain with no check on it at all, held only by somebody remembering. `release-ready` runs weekly and gives three answers, never two: **ready** when `## Unreleased` has bullets and every check on `main` is green, with the `bump_version` and tag commands in the issue; **not ready** naming the checks that are not green; and **not known**, which is not ready, when a reading could not be taken. Four refusals come before any verdict, each with its own non-zero exit -- an unreadable `CHANGELOG.md` ("this is not 'nothing to release'"), no release to compare against, checks that could not be read ("whether a release would be safe is NOT KNOWN"), and a `main` with no check runs at all ("an unanswered question") -- because a report that said ready because it could not look would be this project's own defect pointed at its release. It reads EVERY check rather than the eight required ones, and says why in the issue: `gate-ci` is the only job that runs the gate as the real Action, as a Tekton Task in kind and as the GitLab template, and it is not required, so a merge queue is green while it is red. A `skipped` job is not a failure. An empty `## Unreleased` closes the issue instead of nagging, which is what `bump_version` leaves behind after a release took everything. The workflow reports and cannot write to the repository: `contents: read`, `issues: write`, `checks: read`, and a test holds that it runs no `git push`, `git commit` or `git tag`. The counting expression lives in the test as well as the workflow, so a CHANGELOG reshape fails here rather than in a Monday run. The rule for the next writer: a step held together by somebody remembering is not held together -- and when the thing you publish is read from the newest release, the cadence is part of the product | `tests/test_a_release_nobody_cuts.py::test_the_cadence_is_checked_weekly_and_can_be_asked_by_hand`, `tests/test_a_release_nobody_cuts.py::test_the_awk_counts_only_what_is_waiting_to_be_released`, `tests/test_a_release_nobody_cuts.py::test_every_check_counts_not_only_the_required_eight`, `tests/test_a_release_nobody_cuts.py::test_a_reading_that_could_not_be_taken_is_not_ready`, `tests/test_a_release_nobody_cuts.py::test_nothing_to_release_closes_the_issue_instead_of_nagging`, `tests/test_a_release_nobody_cuts.py::test_the_ready_answer_carries_the_command_and_the_not_ready_answer_carries_the_reason`, `tests/test_a_release_nobody_cuts.py::test_it_cannot_write_to_the_repository_itself`, `tests/test_a_release_nobody_cuts.py::test_the_real_changelog_is_readable_by_the_same_expression` |

| G360 | **A registry entry is a claim about where a kind keeps its containers, and a claim is checked against what the reader read, not taken from the kind's name.** `unread_pod_specs` is the floor under the kind registries: a container in a kind with no reader withholds the verdict (G232, G333, G355). For a kind a registry names it returned `[]` without looking -- "for those kinds the registry is the claim" -- and three guards in `rules.py`, one in `verify`, one in the control-plane rules and one in the posture scan each repeated the decision as `kind not in WORKLOAD_KINDS`. Measured on 0.10.0 with the CLI: a `DeploymentConfig` with a privileged container under `template.Spec` (capital S) scored 92.0 to 96.0, gated `passed`, verified `allow`; an `InferenceService` with a privileged `spec.explainer.alibi` scored 79.0, `passed`, `allow`; a `RayCluster` and an `InferenceService` with a pod spec at a path the registry does not list scored 80.0, `passed`, `allow`. **What this was not:** a deployable bypass. Not walked, because each holds a string `image` that is not a container: inside a container the reader read, the `volumes` of a pod spec the reader read (`rbd.image`), and the object's top-level `status` (`containerStatuses[].image` in anything read back from a cluster). The reason is a second fixed phrase, `holds a container where kube-noob does not read one in this kind`, because "pods are in a kind kube-noob has no reader for" is false of a kind that has one and ends by telling the reader to ask for it to be added. It travels as a `notAudited` row, so every renderer that reads the row carries it; the posture scan has its own reader and its own guard and was asked separately. Fifteen neighbouring shapes that gated `passed` on 0.10.0 are held beside the three (review class 21), with a core `CronJob` that KN-V007 was already failing as the sixteenth, and the test that pinned the old decision, `test_a_kind_the_registries_do_read_is_unaffected`, is flipped and renamed with the reasoning in its docstring. **Not covered, and stated so nobody reads this row as closing the class:** a workload object with no pod spec anywhere (`DeploymentConfig` with no `template`, `InferenceService` with `spec: {}`) still scores -- that is an absence, a core `Deployment` with no template behaves the same, and a valid `RayJob` that selects an existing cluster is exactly that shape; and a container that declares no `image` under a key no registry names is not detected, for the | G359 | **A receipt reads the verdict the report carries; it must not read a key the report stopped writing, and it must not recompute an exit code the command already decided (review classes 4, 9 and 21).** G310's rule is that a receipt records the verdict it was given and never re-judges it. The rule held and the report moved: `receipt.audit_exit_code`, which `audit` returns, read `summary.scoreWithheld`, the name G272 gave the undetermined reason and G281 retired for `summary.verdict: undetermined` + `summary.verdictReason`. The key was then absent, absent read as "not withheld", and an undetermined audit with nothing at `--fail-on` would have exited 0 with a receipt recording 0 and a null score beside no reason. `receipt.audit_undetermined` reads the two keys the report writes, and the word decides: `undetermined` with no reason is still exit 1. For the gate, `receipt.gate_exit_code` is the ONE place the code is decided -- returned by `gate`, recorded by the receipt, recomputed by `receipt verify` -- read off `model.open_unread` (G340), `model.blocked_manifests` (G325) and `passed`; before, the receipt was written ahead of the G325 branch and recorded 1 for a run that exited 5, and the replay carried its own arithmetic and dropped `--skip-unreadable`, so a waived run verified as DIFFERS. The reason a gate receipt records is `model.incomplete_reason`, so all three ways a gate is incomplete are named and the word beside it is `gate.verdict_word`'s. The receipt field is `verdict.undetermined` (was `scoreWithheld`, never released). A gate receipt's `assumptions` and `exceptedFindings` are read off the report rather than written as empty. The receipt documents are renderers of the verdict and have adapters in the conformance suite; `verify_receipt` states no verdict over its input and is exempted with what it owes. | G364 | **A conformance matrix whose every withheld object stands alone proves the easy half; the mixture -- clean objects beside one object nothing read -- is the half that lies, and the lie is in the figures, not the verdict.** G363 was found by a fixture the matrix did not have, and it had one such fixture afterwards. The webhook adapter was sent only the FIRST object of a fixture, so its conformance depended on which file sorted first -- with the Task last it was handed a clean NetworkPolicy, carried nothing and read as clean; it is now sent every object, one request each, and a per-object test holds the warning to the object that was not read. Run through every surface with the real CLI, the mixture found that the verdict word is right everywhere G363 left it and that the FIGURES beside it are computed over a denominator that excludes the unread object: `controlSummary` `clean` 88 with and without the unread Task, "42 rules evaluated", KSI `clean` 190 and "31 indicators evidenced and clean", the receipt's `controlCoverage`, a snapshot's `evaluatedRules`, and `posture diff` listing a critical finding as **fixed** when its object merely stopped being read. Those are NOT fixed here, on purpose: the fix for each is "pass the unread rows in and subtract", it is owed in nine places after the three G343 and G363 already patched, and `clean` is in no report to fix once -- every consumer re-derives it (class 25 with no predicate to move). `docs/report-ir.md` records the measurement, the minimal coverage ledger that makes `clean` a recorded fact, and the order to land it in. The two predicates in the matrix do not hold figures, and its docstring now says so rather than letting a green run imply it. | `tests/test_renderer_conformance.py::test_the_matrix_holds_the_mixture_and_not_only_the_object_alone`, `tests/test_renderer_conformance.py::test_renderer_conformance`, `tests/test_renderer_conformance.py::test_every_fixture_has_the_shape_it_claims`, `tests/test_renderer_conformance.py::test_the_webhook_warns_about_the_object_nothing_read_and_not_about_its_clean_neighbour`, `tests/test_renderer_conformance.py::test_a_rule_whose_only_subject_was_refused_does_not_say_the_kind_is_absent`, `tests/test_renderer_conformance.py::test_an_accepted_unreadable_file_does_not_accept_the_refusal_beside_it`, `tests/test_renderer_conformance.py::test_the_adapters_cover_the_renderers_the_seven_defects_were_found_in` |

| G366 | **A finding that stopped being provable is not a finding that was fixed, and the guard that says so has to be an OBJECT, not a kind (review classes 1, 9 and 25).** `diff` already refused to call an object `removed` when its whole kind went unread -- the RBAC regression that published a lost `list networkpolicies` as a security improvement -- and applied the same guard to findings. The guard was `notReadKinds`, a set of KINDS, and a kind is the wrong granularity: move one Deployment to an apiVersion the rules refuse while another Deployment in the same input is still read, and `Deployment` never enters `notReadKinds` at all, so the refused object's findings fall straight through. The negative control is the point of the join rather than a blanket refusal: an object still read and no longer privileged is remediation and still reads as `fixed`. This is the delta form of the product rule, and the first consumer of the coverage contract `docs/report-ir.md` proposes. Held by tests that drive `gate.sh` itself rather than re-deriving its logic, because the logic was never the defect, plus two that hold the corrected sentence -- a doc fix with no test is one the next edit reverts. | G378 | **The one instruction the project gives an operator for verifying a release cannot be carried out where that operator is (review classes 1 and 24).** `SECURITY.md` told a reader to run `gh attestation verify --repo kube-noob/kube-noob` and keyless `cosign verify`, and said nothing about what they need. Both need a network: the first asks GitHub's attestations API for the bundle, the second needs Sigstore's trust root (Fulcio and Rekor). kube-noob is offline-first, its `airgap` CI job proves the install and four workflows run inside a namespace with no interface and no route, and the operators it is built for are behind an air gap -- so the instruction's precondition is absent in exactly the environment the product exists for, and the document presented it as though it held. Measured on the v0.10.0 release: the assets are the wheel, the sdist, the chart, the krew archive, the skill zip, `sbom.spdx.json` and twelve demo GIFs, and a query for any asset whose name contains `bundle`, `sigstore`, `intoto` or `att` returns **zero** -- so the offline forms both tools support (`gh attestation verify --bundle`, which its own help calls "offline verification using attestations stored on disk", and `cosign verify --offline`) have nothing on the release to point at. An operator with no network has the artefact, the SBOM, and no way to check either. The section now says which commands need a network, what to fetch on a connected machine and carry across, and states plainly that until the bundles are published as release assets an air-gapped operator cannot verify a release from the release alone -- the honest half, said rather than left silent, which is G367's lesson applied to this project's own supply chain rather than to a CI gate. It also separates the two trust claims that are easy to merge: the evidence receipts are digest-bound and replayable, are not signatures, and `receipt verify` needs no network, which is a different question from whether the released artefact is the one that was published. The two other pairings were weighed and rejected for the same reason: with both snapshots withheld, or with the evidence appearing rather than disappearing, nothing moves, the one guard a delta has never runs, and the adapter passes with that guard deleted. **One predicate did not translate as written, and that is the finding of the exercise.** `presents_as_clean` asks whether the document reads as "nothing to see"; for a delta that is answered by `verdict`, and `diff` sets `verdict: undetermined` from the unaccepted `notAudited` rows whether or not G366's join exists -- so the predicate as written passes a `diff` that lists the critical finding as `fixed`. The smallest honest translation is the same question asked of a ROW: `fixed` and `removed` are the delta's two affirmative claims, and one of them about an object the same report names under `notAudited` is the consumer concluding "nothing to see" about that object. Measured with the join reverted (patch saved, reverted, run, reapplied): **38 findings `fixed` across five of the matrix's fixtures, about five objects those same reports name under `notAudited`, KN-S001 critical among them in three, with headlines reading `13 fixed`, `12 fixed`, `10 fixed`, `2 fixed` and `1 fixed`** -- ten matrix cells and the named row test red, 14 failures with the three G366 tests; green, every one of them is 0. **The defect adding the adapter found and fixed:** `drift.history`'s `complete` flag, the point a dashboard and the HTML trend draw, was read off `notRead` alone -- the resources a CLUSTER refused. An object the RULES refused puts nothing there, so on `mixed-refused-object-of-the-kind-the-passing-rules-read` the series read `open: 10 -> 0, complete: true` on both days: G366's lie redrawn one surface over (class 25). | G373 | **A reason the product prints is built from what the RULE declares and from fixed phrases, never from the input's own text (review classes 7 and 3).** `kube_noob.audit.rules.refusal` has stated the discipline since G365 -- "the phrase is built from that kind name (a key of `SUBJECT_GROUPS`, never the input's own text) and nothing else, so it can stand in a reason" -- and `policy reach` did not follow it. Its notes and reasons name the policy that decided, and that name came out of `metadata.name` raw. The terminal boundary (`_VisibleStream` -> `sanitize.visible_lines`) keeps the newline on purpose, because it is cleaning multi-line text, so a name holding one put a line of the document's choosing at column zero of kube-noob's own output. Measured on a `ClusterNetworkPolicy` named `payments\nnot audited: 0 object(s); the verdict is a pass\nnote: every ClusterNetworkPolicy was evaluated` with `priority: 9999`, `policy reach --format text` printed three lines where kube-noob wrote one: `  note: ClusterNetworkPolicy/payments` / `not audited: 0 object(s); the verdict is a pass` / `note: every ClusterNetworkPolicy was evaluated: priority 9999 is outside 0-1000, which the API server refuses, so this policy is not evaluated` -- two forged lines, one of them carrying the word pass, and the same name reached `undeterminedBy` and the `Undetermined:` summary; in JSON it arrived as one string with two embedded newlines, which a model reading the result cannot attribute. The same vector was open on a `NetworkPolicy`'s name (the ingress reason: `1 NetworkPolicy (shop/default-deny` / `not audited: ...) selects ...`), on an `AdminNetworkPolicy`'s name, and on a label VALUE in a peer selector, which `describe_selector` echoes in the reason for every denial (`allows all pods in namespaces team=web` / `not audited: ...`). After: `ClusterNetworkPolicy/<payments not audited: 0 object(s); the verdict is a pass note: every ClusterNetworkPolicy was evaluated>: priority 9999 is outside 0-1000, ...` on one line, and `team=<web not audited: 0 object(s); the verdict is a pass>`. The judgement made here explicitly: the value is KEPT, because a note that cannot say which object and which field is wrong is a worse outcome than one that echoes a value -- what changed is that a name that is not a well-formed Kubernetes name is made printable, collapsed to one line, cut at `kube_noob.audit.argv.SHOWN_LIMIT` and wrapped in `<>`, which no valid name contains, so it still identifies the object and can no longer pose as kube-noob's voice. The field VALUES (`priority {priority!r}`, `apiVersion`, `tier`, `action`) were the other classification and were NOT forgeable: `repr` quotes and escapes a newline, measured as `priority '9999\nnot audited: ...'` on one line. They bounded nothing, though -- a 4005-character `tier` printed in full -- and a mapping, a list or a float `nan` came out as Python source rather than as what the document says, so they go through the same bounded quoting and print `null` and `true` rather than `None` and `True`. Two defects found beside it: `cnp.collect` matched the apiVersion with `endswith("/v1alpha2")`, so `example.com/v1alpha2 ClusterNetworkPolicy` -- a vendor CRD sharing only the kind name, which `rules.refusal` does not cover in this tree -- was read with the upstream v1alpha2 semantics and answered `DENIED  Denied by ClusterNetworkPolicy/future-deny ingress r (Admin tier, priority 10).` with a fix addressed to a cluster administrator; it is now compared against the whole string this module declares, which is right whether or not `refusal` comes to cover the kind, and the verdict is undetermined with the apiVersion named. And `sorted(subject)` over a subject mapping with an integer key beside a string key raised `TypeError` and ended the command instead of reporting one unreadable policy. The negative controls are the point: a well-formed policy's verdict, tier, reason string and empty note list are byte-identical to the pre-change code, a 253-character name prints unmarked, and a malformed policy still names the object, the field and the value. Not covered: the bounded helpers are `policy reach`'s (`kube_noob.debug.netpol`, `anp`, `cnp`) and are not yet the one shared pair for the whole product -- `kube_noob.audit.argv._shown`, `kube_noob.operators._shown`, `kube_noob.gate._cell` and `kube_noob.sanitize.one_line` remain four separate bounding conventions; `Endpoint.describe()` and the namespaces in a reason are still printed from the resolved `--from`/`--to` arguments rather than through `shown_name`, which is safe only because those are validated CLI input; and the identical shape in `kube_noob.operator_issues`, `kube_noob.operators` and `kube_noob.cli`'s `receipt` messages was not measured here. | | G376 | **A test that plants a binary inside the working directory asks the product to do the one thing it refuses, and the failure reads as a missing tool (review classes 9 and 24).** `tests/test_for_model.py::test_helm_audit_prints_one_json_document` writes its own fake `helm` -- a shell script that `cat`s a fixture -- so it needs no real Helm at all. It planted it at `tmp_path/bin`, and `tests/conftest.py` makes `tmp_path` the working directory. `helm_binary` looks only in absolute PATH entries outside the project tree, and with no git root the project tree IS the working directory, so the fake is a program from the tree under review offered to the tool reviewing it -- exactly what G280 added that guard to refuse. The command returned **exit 4 with an empty stdout** and the message "The `helm` binary is not on PATH (absolute PATH entries outside the working directory only)", the test failed on `json.loads('')`, and the failure was read for weeks as "helm cannot be installed on this machine" -- a true sentence about the machine and a false explanation of the failure. In CI a system `helm` sits higher on PATH and answers first, so **the fixture's own fake was never exercised there**: the test passed for a reason unrelated to what it claims to test, and failed on every machine without Helm, which is every air-gapped one the product is built for. The binary now goes in a sibling of the working directory, so the fake answers on every machine and the test exercises the mechanism it describes rather than deferring to whatever Helm happens to be installed. The guard is unchanged and is correct: G280 is the rule, this was a fixture asking it to break. Consequence beyond the one test: `scripts/check.sh` could not reach exit 0 on a machine without Helm, so its `notrun` path -- the one written for exactly those machines -- was unreachable, and the full suite had no green baseline to compare against. Not covered: whether any other fixture plants an executable under the working directory, and the `tests/test_coverage_paths.py` case that still branches on `shutil.which("helm")` and so asserts different exit codes on different machines. | `tests/test_for_model.py::test_helm_audit_prints_one_json_document`, `tests/test_for_model.py::test_the_mode_is_deterministic`, `ci:test` |
| G370 | **Two commands re-derived a verdict the report already carried, and disagreed with it (review classes 1, 9 and 25).** `AGENTS.md` publishes one exit-code contract and states that "1 and 5 are not two answers to the same question"; `receipt.audit_exit_code` is the one place for the arithmetic and reads `summary.verdict`. **`validate` did not ask it.** Measured on a hardened Pod beside an `rbac.k8s.io/v1 ClusterRoleBinding` (an apiVersion no API server serves, which the rules refuse): `audit -f mixed/ --fail-on never` exits **1** and `validate -f mixed/` exited **0**, after printing `verdict: undetermined` with `verdictReason: "1 ClusterRoleBinding not audited ..."` in its own report. It returned `1 if high + critical else 0` -- counts that are 0 when the only thing wrong is that an object was not read -- eleven lines below the branch that calls the one predicate. It now asks `receipt.audit_undetermined` first, and a readable input still exits 0. **`posture scan --fail-above N` exited 0 on every object the scan held and could not read.** The widest case: a ClusterRoleBinding granting cluster-admin to `system:authenticated` under that same unserved apiVersion gave `paths: []`, `refused: []`, exit **0**, while the command's own `--help` promises "exit 5 when an object or a read was refused ... listed under `refused`, because the gate did not see everything". All three `unmodelled` call sites behaved that way and all three disagree with the audit: the misgrouped ClusterRoleBinding, an `argoproj.io/v1alpha1 Rollout` carrying a **privileged** container (`audit` exit 1, `notAudited: ['Rollout/prod/r']`; scan exit 0, `refused: []`), and a `serving.knative.dev/v1 Route` or an `example.com/v1 Role`. Those last two are the ones that look as though they should stay ungated, and measuring `rules.refusal` on them is what decided otherwise -- it returns a reason for each, so `audit` already exits 1 on a bundle holding one, and the scan was the outlier. So the rule is agreement with the audit: the scan withholds a pass for exactly the objects the audit does not audit, and `unmodelled` is gated. The reason given for leaving it ungated -- "a CRD the cluster does not serve or a built-in role the bundle does not carry is normal" -- describes `absent`, whose single call site is "is not in the input", which stays ungated; a present-but-unread object had been filed with the genuinely absent ones, which is G368's finding in a third module. Each of the three sites already said in its own comment that the object mattered ("It still opens the RBAC leg: an RBAC-shaped object this scan could not read"; "its namespace's exposure is not determined rather than none"; "dropping them unmentioned reported '0 workloads' for a file full of Argo Rollouts") before choosing the cause the gate ignores. Negative controls: a correctly grouped binding is in neither `refused` nor `notRead` and exits 0, a clean input exits 0 from both commands, and `--fail-above` absent still does nothing on its own (G353). Not covered: a bundle holding a Knative Route or an Argo Rollout now fails `posture scan --fail-above` as it already failed `audit`, and the escape hatch for both is a `notAudited` exception rather than a quieter scan -- if that is too strict for `--fail-above` the flag needs an opt-out, which is a decision about what it promises; `unplaced` and `ambiguous` name an object whose identity is unsettled and already record a `gap`; and `gate -f dir/` still skips a chart's `templates/` while `verify` exits 5 on the same directory. | `tests/test_the_exit_code_contract_agrees_with_itself.py::test_the_premise_audit_calls_this_input_undetermined_and_exits_one`, `tests/test_the_exit_code_contract_agrees_with_itself.py::test_validate_says_undetermined_in_the_report_it_prints`, `tests/test_the_exit_code_contract_agrees_with_itself.py::test_validate_no_longer_exits_zero_on_an_undetermined_report`, `tests/test_the_exit_code_contract_agrees_with_itself.py::test_validate_still_exits_zero_on_an_input_it_could_read`, `tests/test_the_exit_code_contract_agrees_with_itself.py::test_validate_and_audit_now_agree_about_this_directory`, `tests/test_the_exit_code_contract_agrees_with_itself.py::test_posture_scan_reports_the_misgrouped_binding_under_refused`, `tests/test_the_exit_code_contract_agrees_with_itself.py::test_posture_scan_no_longer_exits_zero_beside_a_binding_it_could_not_read`, `tests/test_the_exit_code_contract_agrees_with_itself.py::test_posture_scan_still_exits_zero_when_it_read_everything`, `tests/test_the_exit_code_contract_agrees_with_itself.py::test_a_binding_under_the_group_the_scan_reads_is_still_read`, `tests/test_the_exit_code_contract_agrees_with_itself.py::test_fail_above_absent_still_does_nothing_on_its_own`, `tests/test_the_exit_code_contract_agrees_with_itself.py::test_the_gated_causes_are_the_ones_that_name_something_the_input_held`, `tests/test_the_exit_code_contract_agrees_with_itself.py::test_the_audit_refuses_the_two_shapes_that_look_like_they_should_not_gate`, `tests/test_the_exit_code_contract_agrees_with_itself.py::test_the_scan_and_the_audit_agree_about_a_kind_the_scan_does_not_rank`, `tests/test_unread_is_not_a_pass.py::test_every_not_read_site_goes_through_the_rule`, `tests/test_unread_is_not_a_pass.py::test_each_not_read_site_gates_exactly_when_the_rule_says` |
| G377 | **A claim about incompleteness is worth what a stranger can reproduce, so the claim is sixteen inputs built to make a scanner under-report with the honest answer recorded beside each one (review classes 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 7, 9, 13, 14, 21, 23, 24 and 25).** Three external reviews, asked separately what would most increase this product's credibility with a defence buyer, named the same artifact rather than a better sentence. `corpus/` is it: sixteen case directories, each holding the manifests and a `honest-answer.yaml` recording what the object is, the verdict word and exit code kube-noob's own output gives, what the answer must NAME, and one sentence on the mechanism by which a tool that does not model its own incompleteness reports less than is there -- phrased about the mechanism and never about a named product, which `tests/test_the_adversarial_corpus.py::test_no_mechanism_sentence_names_another_product` enforces. **There is deliberately no competitor scoreboard**, and `corpus/README.md` says why in its own sentence: a league table invites an argument about the table, a reproducible corpus does not. The cases are the measured defects of the last three days turned into inputs anyone can run -- a `metadata.name` carrying two real newlines and the word "pass" (01), a `projectcalico.org/v3 NetworkPolicy` beside a readable Deployment (02), `cluster-admin` bound to `system:authenticated` under `rbac.k8s.io/v1`, one segment short of the real group (03), a `ClusterNetworkPolicy` under a foreign group (04), a refused object beside a clean one (05), an object that stops being readable between two snapshots (06), a privileged container in the 201st template of an Argo `Workflow` (07), a `DeploymentConfig` whose container sits under `spec.template.Spec` (08), an `argoproj.io/v1alpha1 Rollout` (09), two `securityContext` keys on one container (10), a privileged `securityContext` reached through a YAML alias defined in the annotations (11), `extensions/v1beta1` on a Deployment (12), `hostNetwork`, `hostPID` and the host root inside an operator CRD's own pod template (13), a selectorless LoadBalancer Service with a hand-written EndpointSlice (14), a duplicate `items` key on the `List` wrapping a refused object (15), and a selectorless Service beside a privileged host-mounting pod (16). Measured with the real CLI at 0.10.0 before anything was written down: every case answers honestly today -- 01, 10, 11 and 12 `failed` exit 1; 02, 03, 04, 05, 07, 08, 09, 13 and 15 `incomplete` exit 1 with the object named under `notAudited`; 06 `undetermined` exit 1 with 15 findings carried to `findings.undetermined` and 0 to `fixed`; 14 and 16 `incomplete` exit 5 under `posture scan --require-complete`. The forged name is flattened at the output boundary on every surface tried (`audit`, `gate`, `verify`, `posture scan`, text and JSON), the markdown table escapes a `|` in it, and no line of output begins with a word the name supplied -- including `U+2028`, which `str.splitlines` treats as a line break and a `\n`-only sanitiser would not. `scripts/corpus_run.py` is the runner and the documented command a stranger runs; it reads each command's own verdict word out of that command's own output rather than deriving one of its own (class 9), finds the subcommand by name because `--format json gate` is not the command `json` (class 22), runs each case twice and compares the bytes, reads no clock, network or cluster, and reports a disagreement instead of accommodating it -- the record is never edited to match the tool. **What the corpus found that was not already known:** case 16, measured, `kube-noob posture scan -f manifests` on a selectorless LoadBalancer Service beside a privileged pod with `hostPID` and the host root mounted, labelled for that Service and listening on its target port, exits **0** with the headline `0 attack paths from 1 exposures; top: no path from an exposure to a node or to API rights` -- a zero that is the absence of a resolution standing where the absence of a path would stand, with `workloads: 1` in the same document. The Service IS named on the next line and in `notRead`, and `--require-complete` exits 5, so the caveat is present and the honest answer is reachable; what is wrong is the headline and the `summary` field, which keeps that sentence verbatim even at `--require-complete`, and which the MCP tool `posture_scan` hands its caller whole (`toolkit.posture_scan` returns `attack_paths.scan_manifests`'s document unaltered). The mechanism is one line: `attack_paths.py:2779` writes the `not determined:` prefix only `if refused`, and `GATED_CAUSES` is `frozenset({"refused"})`, so of the five `UnreadCause` values only a refusal reaches the headline -- which is right for `absent` (a type the cluster does not serve, G293) and wrong for `ambiguous` and `unplaced`, which bear directly on the counts the headline states. Recorded under `open_defect` in case 16 rather than asserted, so the commit that fixes it does not fight the corpus. Not covered: that headline is NOT fixed here -- choosing which of the five `UnreadCause` values are headline-bearing is a product decision across every renderer that quotes `summary`, and 68 lines of the test suite mention the phrase, so it is not the small fix this entry could hold; After: all seven decline with the refusal phrase and name the object (`1 <Kind> not audited (apiVersion names a group kube-noob does not know <Kind> under)`), KN-C009 is `notEvaluated` with the PDB named, the verdict stays undetermined and the exit code 1. Fix the field" for a field of the wrong type, which is what that sentence is about. | G379 | **An exception that accepts an object nothing read bought a cleaner answer than not having the object at all (review classes 8, 9 and 21).** `AGENTS.md` says a waiver is never a cleaner answer about the same evidence. Measured on `78b0484` with the real CLI at `--fail-on medium`, it was: a hardened three-replica Deployment with probes and no NetworkPolicy exited **1** with score **94.0** and KN-N001 open; the same Deployment beside an allow-all `projectcalico.org/v3 NetworkPolicy` exited 1 with the score withheld, which is right; and the same two files plus a `notAudited` exception accepting that policy exited **0** with score **98.0** and NO findings. Adding an object the rules cannot read, then waiving it, scored four points higher than the cleaner input and passed a gate the cleaner input fails. Before G368 the three inputs gave 94 / withheld / 94-with-KN-N001-open, so this is a regression G368 and G369 introduced rather than a standing defect. The seam is in `_all_accepted` (`audit/model.py`), written for G281 review 5: `Report.undetermined` stops a decline withholding the score once an exception accepts every object the rule declined on. That was sound while a decline only ever meant "the rule found nothing there because it did not look". G368 and G369 made a decline able to stand IN FOR a finding -- KN-N001 now declines instead of reporting "No NetworkPolicy for namespace prod", because the namespace may be isolated by a policy nothing could read -- and the acceptance then re-opened the score while the suppressed finding stayed suppressed, scoring the rule as though it had been evaluated and found nothing. Accepting an object says the reader may stop asking to read it; it does not say what the rule would have found in it. So `AuditContext.finding_suppressed` records the rules whose decline REPLACED a finding, at the eight in-rule call sites that decline instead of yielding, `engine.py` writes `suppressedFinding: true` on those declined rows only (absent reads as "no", which is what every decline meant before this key existed), and `_all_accepted` returns False for such a row however the object was accepted: the report stays undetermined, which is the true answer and neither the one the old code gave nor the one before G368. The distinction is per decline, not per rule and not per report, because the accept path is the only way a Calico, Istio or Knative shop ever reaches a verdict and blunting it would push every such user onto a flag that turns the check off -- the positive control is a refused `dra.k8s.io/v1 ResourceClaim` no rule reasons from the absence of, whose waiver still restores the score to 98.0 and exit 0, unchanged. The two methods moved from `rules.py` to `audit/evidence.py` as module-level functions with thin delegates left behind, because the new field put `rules.py` over the plugin directory's 256 KiB per-file cap. Not covered: a decline the engine records for a rule that never reached its own call site is still coverable by an acceptance, which is correct but means the marking lives at the call sites rather than in the IR, and the coverage ledger `docs/report-ir.md` proposes -- where the obligation, not the object, is the key -- would carry this distinction as data instead. | `tests/test_a_waiver_is_never_a_cleaner_answer.py::test_the_cleaner_input_is_the_baseline`, `tests/test_a_waiver_is_never_a_cleaner_answer.py::test_an_unreadable_policy_alone_withholds_the_verdict`, `tests/test_a_waiver_is_never_a_cleaner_answer.py::test_a_waiver_does_not_buy_a_better_answer_than_the_cleaner_input`, `tests/test_a_waiver_is_never_a_cleaner_answer.py::test_the_waived_report_is_undetermined_rather_than_clean`, `tests/test_a_waiver_is_never_a_cleaner_answer.py::test_the_suppressing_decline_is_marked_as_such`, `tests/test_a_waiver_is_never_a_cleaner_answer.py::test_a_waiver_over_a_decline_that_suppressed_nothing_still_restores_the_score`, `tests/test_a_waiver_is_never_a_cleaner_answer.py::test_that_decline_is_not_marked_as_suppressing` |
| G380 | **The plugin submitted to the directory was the one a community organization is not permitted to publish, and every refusal was a policy category rather than a defect (review class 21: fix the neighbours).** Submission after submission of `plugin/kube-noob` came back refused, and the findings were read as bugs to fix. They are not. The directory accepts a `command` hook, an MCP server that runs on the user's computer, and files that are not Markdown, text, JSON, YAML or an allowed image **only from Verified organizations**, and `kube-noob/kube-noob` is not one. `plugin/kube-noob` is made of exactly those things: `hooks/hooks.json` declares a `PreToolUse` command hook, `.mcp.json` declares a stdio server started with `uv run`, and the folder carries 118 `.py` files, `uv.lock`, `pyproject.toml`, an `.svg` and three `.tmpl` files. Measured against the findings one at a time, each names a category with no fix in it: remove the hook, remove the local server, remove the code -- at which point the plugin is no longer that plugin. The hook command also holds four `<` characters (`exec 3<&0`, `<&3`, two `</dev/null`) against a limit of three, and a `uv run` launcher can be held by policy even pinned exactly, so two further findings are unreachable behind the first three. `plugin/kube-noob-skills` already existed and already clears all of it: the same seven skills driving the CLI the user installs themselves, eleven files, no hook, no server, no code, `claude plugin validate` clean. The fix is therefore which folder is submitted, recorded in `docs/operations.md` so the next person does not re-litigate it, plus the hardening that command does not check and that was done by hand here: every text file ASCII or UTF-8 with no NUL byte, no file beginning `#!`, the icon a real 512x512 PNG and not an archive wearing the name, the largest file 15,678 bytes against 256 KiB and 11 files against 512, no `CLAUDE.md` or `AGENTS.md` name, nothing reading an environment variable or a machine path for a credential, and `NAME`/`NS`/`POD` in fenced example commands in place of angle-bracket placeholders so no extracted command can trip the three-`<` rule. The tests are the gate, because a directory policy is exactly the kind of constraint a later commit re-breaks without noticing: they assert the shape of the published folder rather than re-deriving the policy. Both folders stay -- `plugin/kube-noob` is what ships once the organization is Verified, and it is what the root `marketplace.json` offers to anyone adding this repository as a marketplace directly, a path with no directory policy in it. Not covered: Verification itself is an account action no commit can perform, and until it happens the MCP server and the pre-action hook reach users only through the marketplace, `pipx`, `brew` or `krew`, which `docs/install.md` already documents. | `tests/test_the_plugin_a_community_org_may_publish.py::test_the_published_plugin_is_markdown_a_manifest_and_an_icon`, `tests/test_the_plugin_a_community_org_may_publish.py::test_no_file_in_it_begins_with_a_shebang`, `tests/test_the_plugin_a_community_org_may_publish.py::test_every_text_file_is_utf_8_with_no_nul_byte`, `tests/test_the_plugin_a_community_org_may_publish.py::test_the_icon_is_a_png_and_not_an_archive`, `tests/test_the_plugin_a_community_org_may_publish.py::test_it_declares_no_hook_and_no_mcp_server`, `tests/test_the_plugin_a_community_org_may_publish.py::test_it_holds_no_package_manager_file_and_no_lockfile`, `tests/test_the_plugin_a_community_org_may_publish.py::test_no_fenced_command_holds_three_angle_brackets`, `tests/test_the_plugin_a_community_org_may_publish.py::test_it_is_within_the_file_count_and_file_size_limits`, `tests/test_the_plugin_a_community_org_may_publish.py::test_the_marketplace_still_offers_both_plugins` |
| G382 | **The one command that rewrites a manifest exited 0 beside its own critical finding, and called refused input a finding (review classes 9, 13, 21 and 25).** `fix` is the only command that writes a file for a pipeline to apply, so its exit code is what stands between `kube-noob fix -o out.yaml` and `kubectl apply -f out.yaml`. Both of its branches ended in an unconditional `return 0`. Measured on origin/main at 462f06e over a Deployment with `hostNetwork: true`, `hostPID: true` and `securityContext.privileged: true`: `fix` closed two findings (KN-S008, KN-S003), left `privileged: true` and `hostPID: true` in the bytes it wrote, printed `after.maxSeverity: critical`, `after.score: 23.0` and `after.counts: {critical: 1, high: 2, medium: 7, low: 4}` in its own output -- and exited 0. `audit` on the very file `fix` had just written exits 1 with 13 findings, KN-S001 `Privileged container` among them. Measured on a second input, a Deployment beside an `argoproj.io/v1alpha1 Rollout` the rules do not read: `fix` printed `after.verdict: undetermined` with `verdictReason: "... 1 Rollout not audited ..."` and exited 0, where `audit` on the same input exits 1 -- and the high and critical counts there are both 0, so nothing but the verdict word could have caught it. AGENTS.md is explicit: 1 is "findings at or above `--fail-on`, or a verdict that is undetermined ... never 0 for undetermined, whatever `--fail-on` says (G272, G281)". This is G370's defect in the branch DIRECTLY BELOW G370's own fix: that row moved `validate` onto the shared predicate and left its neighbour in the same `if` chain deriving nothing at all. `kit.fix` calls `analyze()` on the rewritten document and keeps only `as_dict()["summary"]`, which is exactly the shape `receipt.audit_undetermined` reads -- it looks at `summary.verdict` and nothing else -- so the fix is to call that same predicate rather than copy its arithmetic, and the evidence was already in hand one line above the `return 0`. The exit is now derived once, before the output branches, so it no longer depends on `--format` or on whether `-o` was given; both of those were separate branches of one decision and only one was reached per invocation. Beside it: `FixRefused` returned 1, and 1 means findings. A file the API server itself refuses -- `metadata.name` twice -- has no findings, because nothing read it; AGENTS.md gives that 4, "a kube-noob error, or refused input", and it is now 4. Negative controls: a hardened Pod still exits 0, matching `audit` on the same input, and low and medium findings alone do not flip it, matching `audit`'s own default of high plus critical; the rewritten document is still written when the exit is 1, and the two changes are still reported. Two tests in `tests/test_wrong_types.py` asserted `rc == 0` as a stand-in for "did not crash" on documents `fix` cannot read; their subject is the untouched document and the stated reason, so they now assert `rc != 4`, the code that would mean that subject failed. Those same inputs reach 1 through their remaining high findings and not through an undetermined verdict, and that is correct rather than a gap: measured with the pod spec held identical and only the type of `metadata` varying across `7`, `[a, b]` and `"web"`, all three give 16 findings where a readable `metadata` gives 14, the object is carried as `Deployment/<unnamed>`, the unreadable metadata is named, and `gate --fail-on critical` fails on all three. The workload was audited; only its identity was not read, and the two extra findings are about that. An earlier draft of this row called it a gap in `analyze` on the strength of a measurement whose three fixtures did not hold the pod spec constant -- the int one carried `hostNetwork` and `privileged` and the others did not -- so the asymmetry was in the fixtures, not the product (class 8). | `tests/test_the_one_command_that_changes_a_manifest.py::test_fix_does_not_exit_zero_beside_its_own_critical_finding`, `tests/test_the_one_command_that_changes_a_manifest.py::test_fix_agrees_with_audit_on_the_file_fix_itself_wrote`, `tests/test_the_one_command_that_changes_a_manifest.py::test_an_undetermined_after_verdict_is_never_zero`, `tests/test_the_one_command_that_changes_a_manifest.py::test_fix_and_audit_agree_on_an_undetermined_input`, `tests/test_the_one_command_that_changes_a_manifest.py::test_refused_input_is_four_not_one`, `tests/test_the_one_command_that_changes_a_manifest.py::test_a_clean_manifest_still_exits_zero`, `tests/test_the_one_command_that_changes_a_manifest.py::test_the_exit_code_does_not_depend_on_the_output_format`, `tests/test_the_one_command_that_changes_a_manifest.py::test_the_exit_code_does_not_depend_on_whether_output_went_to_a_file`, `tests/test_the_one_command_that_changes_a_manifest.py::test_the_fixed_document_is_still_written_when_the_exit_is_one` |
| G381 | **A KEV catalog that was read and holds nothing said it was never given, and one declaring more entries than were read described itself by the size of the read (review classes 1, 4, 13 and 24).** `Kev.describe()` opened `if not self.entries: return "no KEV catalog given"`. Measured on a valid CISA catalog with `catalogVersion: 2026.11.01`, `dateReleased: 2026-11-01`, `count: 0` and `vulnerabilities: []`: `kev.parse(...).describe()` returned `no KEV catalog given` -- the phrase class 24 forbids for an object that was present. The branch was also redundant: `vuln/assess.py` prints `no KEV catalog given (--kev)` itself when no catalog was loaded, so `describe()` only ever runs on a catalog that WAS given, and the one state it could not express is the only one it was reached for. A reader told nobody supplied a catalog concludes that supplying one would tell them more; the truth is that they did, and it is empty, so no finding in the run could have matched it. `Kev` now carries `given`, set by `parse` and by nothing else, and the three states are distinct: `no KEV catalog given`, `CISA KEV 2026.11.01 (2026-11-01): read and holds no entries, so no finding here can match it`, and the previous sentence for a catalog with entries. Beside it, a second absence in the same read: `parse` walked past every entry that is not a mapping or carries no well-formed CVE id with a bare `continue`, and never compared what it read against the `count` the catalog declares for itself. Measured on a catalog declaring `count: 1200` holding one parseable entry, one malformed id and one integer, the old code answered `CISA KEV 2026.01.01 (2026-01-01), 1 CVEs` -- the size of what was read standing where the size of the catalog belongs, with 1199 entries and two skipped rows unmentioned. `parse` now counts the rows it walks past in `skipped` and records a declared `count` in `declared_count`, and the description names the shortfall. The declared count is input, so it is believed only as a non-negative integer at or below `MAX_ENTRIES` and never as a bool, a float or a string; a declared count BELOW what was read reports no shortfall rather than a negative one, and an absent `count` is not a declared zero. Negative controls: a well-formed catalog's description is byte-identical to the previous code's, and `get` resolves the same CVEs, case-insensitively, as before. Not covered, and larger than this row: the vulnerability lane still answers a zero where it read nothing. Measured on origin/main at 462f06e, `gate -f pod.yaml --scanner-report trivy.json --kev kev.json --fail-on critical` over a Pod declaring two images, with one Trivy report carrying `CreatedAt: 2021-01-01` and `Results: []` and no report at all for the second image, prints `## kube-noob gate passed` and exits 0; the vulnerability block lists only the scanned image, `incomplete` is `false`, and neither the scan date nor the zero-package read reaches the verdict. `vuln/reports.py` extracts no scan or database timestamp from any format and `gate` has no notion of an image without a report. That is the next row, not this one. | `tests/test_a_catalog_that_was_read_and_holds_nothing.py::test_a_catalog_nobody_gave_says_so`, `tests/test_a_catalog_that_was_read_and_holds_nothing.py::test_a_catalog_that_was_read_and_holds_nothing_does_not_claim_it_was_never_given`, `tests/test_a_catalog_that_was_read_and_holds_nothing.py::test_a_catalog_with_entries_names_its_version_date_and_size`, `tests/test_a_catalog_that_was_read_and_holds_nothing.py::test_a_catalog_that_declares_more_than_was_read_says_how_many_were_not`, `tests/test_a_catalog_that_was_read_and_holds_nothing.py::test_every_entry_that_cannot_match_is_counted_not_dropped`, `tests/test_a_catalog_that_was_read_and_holds_nothing.py::test_a_catalog_that_declares_no_count_claims_no_shortfall`, `tests/test_a_catalog_that_was_read_and_holds_nothing.py::test_a_count_that_is_not_a_plausible_integer_is_not_believed`, `tests/test_a_catalog_that_was_read_and_holds_nothing.py::test_a_declared_count_below_what_was_read_never_reports_a_negative_shortfall`, `tests/test_a_catalog_that_was_read_and_holds_nothing.py::test_lookup_is_unchanged_by_any_of_this` |

| G391 | **Two commands printed in the docs could not run, and the only guard over them walked the longest real prefix of the words -- the same one-directional check G237 named and that this repair finally replaces (review classes 8 and 21).** `docs/training.md:8` printed `kube-noob corpus jira --online --project ROX --min-version 4.10 --target 1200`; the command takes `--projects` and `--max-requests`, has no per-version filter at all, and the line exited 2 on a reader's first paste. `docs/website-updates.md:48` cited `posture history --json` as a row's source of truth twice over -- in the prose and in the source cell -- and `history` is not a `posture` subcommand but a KEY in `posture report`'s output (`drift.py` returns `{**report, "history": history(state_dir, keep)}`), and there is no `--json` flag either, only `--format json`. **Why nothing caught them:** the docs hold two different claims and one check cannot carry both. A line in a fenced block is an instruction a reader copies, so it has to parse. A command in backticks in a sentence is a reference -- `kube-noob audit` names the command and leaving `-f` off is correct English -- so demanding a runnable line there would force a rewrite of over a hundred correct sentences, which is why the old guard settled for a prefix and let `vuln rank` and `posture history` through. **The repair:** `tests/test_a_command_we_print_is_a_command_that_runs.py` makes the two claims two checks over `README.md` and every `docs/*.md`. Fenced lines go to the real `build_parser()` and any exit the parser calls an error is the finding (152 lines). Inline references are walked word by word down the subparser tree and only an unknown NAME is a finding (113 references), which catches `posture history` while passing `kube-noob audit`. Neither check executes anything: the parser is asked to parse, so a line that would read a cluster is as safe to check as `--help`. A line with a placeholder, a pipe, a glob or a shell expansion is not one command's worth of words and is skipped, so a floor on each scan's size (150 and 100, measured) fails the suite if an extractor quietly stops matching, and `test_both_checks_can_still_refuse` drives a known-bad argv through each helper so a `return None` dropped into either one fails rather than turning 286 green cases into 286 green cases -- both mutations measured failing, the restore measured green. **The one exemption** is `kube-noob vuln rank` in `docs/guidance.md`, quoted by G237 as a subcommand that does not exist; it is keyed by command text rather than line number, since guidance.md grows at the bottom, and `test_no_exemption_outlives_its_reason` fails if that name is ever dropped from the docs or ever becomes real. | `docs/training.md`, `docs/website-updates.md`, `tests/test_a_command_we_print_is_a_command_that_runs.py` | G206, G235, G237, G386 |
| G392 | **Eleven exit codes the published tables never mentioned, an exemption that was simply false, and a check that pooled every skill's rows so one table could drop a code and stay green (review classes 8, 9, 13 and 25).** G386 added one input per documented exit code and asserted equality, which closed the direction from the table to the code. The other direction stayed open, and it is the one G206 and G237 keep finding. **What the sweep measured.** Handing each command a named file that no parser accepts returns `4`, and handing it a DIRECTORY holding one readable object and one unreadable file returns `5` -- it read something, so it is not refusing the input; it refuses to answer about the rest on evidence it knows is partial. That is uniform across `audit`, `validate`, `fix`, `protocol` and `policy reach`, and the `4` reaches `posture scan` and `posture reach` too. Every published row said `0` and `1`. Both `posture` rows did carry a `5`, and both made it conditional -- "with `--fail-above`", "with `--require-complete`" -- while a plain `posture scan -f <dir>` with no flag at all returns 5, so the condition was narrower than the code's reach. A gate reading any of these rows meets a number its table has never heard of and has to guess, which is the one thing this product exists to refuse. **The false exemption.** `("ticket", 0)` was exempt with the reason that the local Jira copy needs `corpus jira --online`, which an offline test must not do. `sources.jira.sync_projects` takes its client, so a fake one seeds the copy with no network at all and the 0 is driven like every other code -- measured. The stale-exemption check could not see it, because it asks whether the ROW still documents the code and never whether the REASON is true. The list is now empty and `test_the_exemption_list_is_empty` keeps it that way, so re-opening it is a deliberate edit to a test rather than a quiet dict entry. **The pooling defect, found by mutation against my own repair.** The first version of the new check read the codes from `_documented()`, which unions the rows of all seven skills. Deleting the whole `4`/`5` clause from `kubernetes-core/SKILL.md` left the suite GREEN, because `openshift-platform` still spelled it out -- a reader has one SKILL.md in front of them, so a code one skill omits is undocumented for everyone using that skill. `_by_skill()` now keeps the rows per file; the pooled view survives for the coverage direction only, where a code documented anywhere does need an input. The same mutation now fails. **Why it cannot be neutered.** `test_the_refused_input_codes_are_in_the_rows` names the measurement against the tables themselves, per skill, so widening `_UNIVERSAL` or dropping a term from the sweep fails rather than quietly re-opening eleven rows; `_UNIVERSAL` holds only `2` (usage or config: the command never ran) and `141` (closed pipe), which are in the contract once for every command, and explicitly not `4` or `5`, which are answers about the input and change what a gate must do. Three mutations measured failing, the restore measured green. **Two commands no published table routed to at all.** `learn mechanism` -- the mechanism walker, four codes (`0` every observable hop holds, `1` the walk reached the end and a hop is BROKEN, `4` a named input could not be read, `5` the walk STOPPED or a neighbour file could not be read) -- had no row in any of the seven skills, and neither did `learn progress`. The new sweep did not see them either: it walks each skill's rows and asks whether the code is in them, so a command with NO row has no row to walk and is skipped -- an absence reading as nothing to verify, which is the defect this product is named for, inside the check written to stop it. `test_every_command_driven_here_has_a_row_somewhere` closes it, and both rows are now published -- which `test_directory_plugin` then refused twice over, and was right to: a row needs a fixture in `_fixture_argv` so the row's FIELDS are checked against what the command really prints, and it needs prose in that skill that actually runs the command, because a row for a command the instructions never reach is a dead row. Both were added, and the `learn mechanism` fixture is the complete bundle on purpose: the fields a walk that reaches the end prints are not the fields a stopped walk prints. The `learn mechanism` inputs are the contrast worth having: `tests/fixtures/mechanism/complete/bundle.yaml` walks all five observable hops to `0`, `tests/fixtures/mechanism/broken/bundle.yaml` is the same bundle with the Ingress on `nginx` (KN-V019 retires it) for the `1`, and that bundle with ONE unreadable file beside it returns `5` -- a clean walk turned into no answer by an unreadable neighbour, not into a pass. **Left measured and not repaired:** `build audit` and `rhacs compare` return `2` on their own malformed input (a corrupt Containerfile, a truncated policy export) rather than `4`, and `learn submit` scores an unparseable answer `passed: false, score 0.0` with a named `parse` check that failed -- which is the grader's correct verdict on the learner, not an absence read as a zero, so it is not a defect. | `tests/test_one_input_per_documented_exit_code.py`, `plugin/kube-noob-skills/skills/*/SKILL.md` | G206, G237, G382, G384, G386 |
| G393 | **`release-ready` would have named itself as the thing blocking every release, and its first scheduled run had not happened yet, so nothing had ever shown it (review classes 13 and 21).** The workflow reads every check run on main's head and reports a release not ready if any is not green -- deliberately every check, not only the eight required ones, because `gate-ci` is the only job that runs the gate as the real Action, a Tekton Task in kind and the GitLab template, and it is not required. **What nobody checked is that the workflow is in the list it reads.** A run triggered by `schedule` creates a check run on the commit it ran against: measured on this repository, scorecard's scheduled run `45411849676` appears as the `analysis` check run on `a3e40681`, beside the push run's, two entries with the same name and different run ids. `release-ready` runs Mondays on a schedule, so while it is running it is one of those entries, with `conclusion: null` and `status: "in_progress"`. Its own filter is `select((.conclusion // .status) != "success" and .conclusion != "skipped")`: for itself that reads `"in_progress" != "success"` and `null != "skipped"`, both true, so it selected itself. `notgreen` could never be empty, the Ready branch was unreachable, and the weekly issue would have said **Not ready**, naming `in_progress release-ready`, for as long as the workflow existed. The cron is `23 7 * * 1` and the workflow is new, so this had never fired. **The repair** excludes the current run by RUN ID (`/actions/runs/${GITHUB_RUN_ID}/` inside `details_url`), not by name: a name match would also drop a real failure of another job that shares the name -- a manual dispatch overlapping the schedule is somebody else's unfinished answer about main, and dropping it is the absence-reads-as-pass defect this product is named for. If nothing is left after the exclusion the workflow exits 1 saying the answer is NOT KNOWN rather than calling an empty list green. **The test runs the workflow's own lines** -- extracted from the YAML between the exclusion and the line that names what is not green, executed by bash over a crafted `check_runs` list with `GITHUB_RUN_ID` set -- because a test that searched the YAML for the exclusion would pass on an exclusion that does not work, which is exactly the #129 defect where the string `3840` sat in a comment and the deleted size check still passed. Five cases: a green main with the workflow's own run in it reads green; a failing `gate-ci` is still named; another run's `in_progress release-ready` is still named; a check run with no `details_url` is kept; a list holding only this run exits 1 as not known. Two mutations measured failing -- the exclusion removed, and the exclusion rewritten as a name match -- and the restore measured green. **CI caught one thing the branch's own tests did not, because the branch was pushed before its full suite finished:** `test_a_release_nobody_cuts` holds this workflow's four refusals by phrase, and one of them was `no check runs at all` -- a sentence the workflow can no longer truthfully say, since after excluding its own run the list it refuses over is "no check runs OTHER than this workflow's own". The refusal is the same refusal and the exit is the same exit; only the count it is honest about changed, so the phrase moved and the message keeps the project's own words for it ("not a green main -- it is an unanswered question"). The lesson is the ordinary one: a full suite before a push, not beside it. | `.github/workflows/release-ready.yml`, `tests/test_a_report_that_names_itself.py` | G129, G358 |
| G394 | **The one answer that says WHERE a path stops being knowable was reachable from a terminal and nowhere else, and the command held its own copy of the walk (review classes 9 and 25).** `learn mechanism` shipped as a command. The playground runs kube-noob's real tools in a Web Worker and can only call tools BY NAME, which is why the `/learn/mechanisms/` page had nothing to call: the gap was the tool, not the page. **The second source.** `cli.py` did the lesson lookup, the audit and the walk inline, so adding a tool beside it would have made two surfaces computing one verdict separately -- the defect class 9 names. The lookup, the audit and the walk now live in `Toolkit.mechanism` and `cli.py` calls it; the command's own behaviour is unchanged, measured (exit 2 on an unknown id with the known ids named, exit 5 on a stopped walk). **The guard is a comparison, not a grep.** `test_the_command_and_the_tool_give_the_same_answer` runs `kube-noob --format json learn mechanism` in a subprocess and compares the whole dict with the tool's; a test that searched `cli.py` for the call would pass on a reimplementation that drifts. The tool is read-only, measured by comparing the data folder before and after rather than by trusting the flag, and its description carries the rule an agent needs before it calls: `stopped` is neither a pass nor a failure, read `stoppedAt`. **A classification I got wrong first.** `test_every_renderer_has_an_adapter` refuses a renderer with neither an adapter nor a stated exemption, and I adapted it: the walk does state a verdict, so it looked adapted. It failed `test_a_complete_report_is_not_reported_as_withheld`, correctly. The adapter's two predicates are about the INPUT's coverage, and the walk's verdict is about ONE named path: the `fully-evaluated-and-clean` fixture carries no Ingress, no Service and no NetworkPolicy, so the walk stops because that path is genuinely unknowable from those objects, while nothing whatever was withheld about the bundle. "Nothing was withheld about the input" and "this path is not walkable here" are different claims. It is now exempt for the same reason `posture_reach` is, with what it owes instead named in the exemption, as G374 requires. 71 tools now, 64 read-only. **Two more surfaces a new tool has to reach, and the full suite is what names them:** `tool_profiles` carries every tool in the profile whose job it serves, and a tool in none is offered by no profile at all -- `learn_mechanism` had to join `_LEARN`; and the website queue's figures paragraph states the tool count and is asserted against the code, so 57 became 58 there in the same change. Measured on `complete.yaml` with its NetworkPolicy removed: with `partial_bundle=True` the hop `policy-admits-the-packet` is `cannot-judge` ("KN-N001, KN-N007, KN-N012 reached no verdict here: part of a namespace was passed"), the walk is `stopped`, exit 5; with it false the same hop `fails` ("KN-N001 found something on this hop"), the walk is `broken`, exit 1. A reader who pastes one Deployment into the browser playground has not said their namespace lacks a NetworkPolicy, and reporting that hop BROKEN claims it does on evidence that cannot say so -- this product's own rule pointed the other way round, an absence read as a FINDING rather than as a pass. The field now defaults to `true`, as it does on the other tool that takes pasted manifests, because the safe direction is the one that withholds; the command passes `_partial_input` so the walk and the audit of one input agree about what an absence means. | G395 | **A walk in which nothing was checked answered `complete` at exit 0, and a hop's title asserted a path fact its rules never establish (review classes 1, 4 and 13).** Two defects in the mechanism walk, both found by red-teaming it. **The vacuous pass.** A hop marked `observable: false` never sets `stopped_at` and is excluded from `judged`, and nothing required `judged >= 1` for `complete` -- so a lesson whose every hop is unobservable walked `verdict: complete | exitCode: 0 | counts.judged: 0` under the sentence "every observable hop on this path holds", which is *vacuously* true: there are no observable hops, so all of them hold. That is the same failure as the empty `parametrize` reporting one skipped test at exit 0 and the empty list reading as clean, and `docs/learning.md` promised the opposite in the same breath -- "`5` says the path was not walked" and "kube-noob does not claim to have checked something it cannot see, in a lesson any more than in an audit". It was reachable by content pressure rather than by accident: **zero of the 98 rules read the kubelet, the container runtime, cgroups, kube-proxy, iptables, conntrack, veth or the overlay**, so an author writing a Node-layer lesson could either mark its hops observable and watch the walk stop at hop 1 with "no rule in this build reads this hop", or mark them `observable: false` and get a full green page. The repair is both halves: `judged == 0` is now `stopped` at exit 5 with a reason that says no hop was judged, and `stopped_at` stays null on purpose, because no hop was the obstacle -- each was honestly reported `not-observable`, none was skipped, and naming the first would be a false statement about it and would imply the rest were undrawn. The curriculum invariant is the half that stops such a lesson being written: every mechanism lesson must declare at least one observable hop whose `rules` name a rule `audit.engine.list_rules()` really has, and the failure message tells the author to write the rule first and the lesson after. **The over-claiming title.** Hop 4 of `mech-net-request-to-pod` rendered `[holds] Network policy lets the packet in` over a deny-all policy (`podSelector: {}`, `policyTypes: [Ingress]`, no `ingress:` rules, admitting nothing). The `holds` SENTENCE was true -- KN-N001 fires on an absent policy, KN-N007 on an over-permissive one, KN-N012 on an inert one, and all three are clean on deny-all -- but the TITLE claimed admission, which by construction none of them can establish. Auditing every hop of both lessons against the rules' CODE (not their titles: this walk was burned once already by assuming KN-C009 judges a missing PodDisruptionBudget the way KN-N001 judges a missing NetworkPolicy, where measured KN-C009 abstains and KN-N001 fires, G389) found four more, each measured: `edge-admits` held `[holds] The edge admits the request` over an Ingress whose only backend names a Service absent from the bundle, because KN-N003, KN-N009, KN-N011 and KN-V019 read TLS and ingress-nginx hazards and nothing reads host, path or backend; `service-selects-pods` held "on a port the containers declare" over `targetPort: 9999` against a container declaring 8080, because KN-V012 reads a NAMED targetPort only; and both replica hops of `mech-cp-a-node-goes-dark` walked `complete` at exit 0 over ONE replica with no `topologySpreadConstraints` and no `podAntiAffinity`, saying "the workload runs more than a single replica" and "the pod template spreads its replicas" -- KN-C007 is excused by any HorizontalPodAutoscaler naming the workload whatever its own `minReplicas` (1..1 here), and KN-C010 skips a workload below two replicas. That last pair also answers the general question: a hop IS reported `holds` when its rules all abstained silently, because a rule that skips its subject records nothing in `notEvaluated` and nothing in `findings`, so the walk cannot tell it from a rule that read the object and was satisfied -- which is why these hops may claim only what the rules test for. Four hops were retitled and three `holds` sentences narrowed, the smaller change in each case; step `id`s were left alone, because they are `stoppedAt`'s vocabulary and renaming them breaks callers for no gain. `workload-selects-its-own-pods` and `the-budget-lets-one-go` were audited and found sound: KN-V010 and KN-C009 decide exactly what those titles say. **Mutated:** reverting the `judged == 0` branch turns the verdict test red (`complete` != `stopped`); adding a temporary all-unobservable lesson to `curriculum.yaml` turns the invariant red and names it. | `tests/test_a_hop_nobody_read_is_not_a_hop_that_works.py`, `src/kube_noob/learn/mechanism.py`, `src/kube_noob/data/curriculum.yaml`, `docs/learning.md` | G387, G389 |
| G397 | **KN-C007 was excused by an autoscaler that can hold the workload at one replica, and said nothing about it (review classes 1 and 13).** The rule fires on a Deployment, StatefulSet or ReplicaSet declaring fewer than two replicas, and its own rationale is "one replica means downtime on every node drain, upgrade or node failure". `_has_hpa` excused it whenever ANY HorizontalPodAutoscaler's `scaleTargetRef` named the workload, and never read that autoscaler's `minReplicas` -- which the API defaults to 1. | G398 | **A dependency bump turned code scanning red on `main`, and its own pull request had shown it green (review class 13).** Dependabot #127 moved `github/codeql-action` from v4.39.3 to v4.39.4 across three workflows. It was merged after checking the eight required checks, which were green. `analyze` -- CodeQL's own job, not required -- went red on `main` at exactly that commit, and on #135 the same day; it had been green at the commit before, and green on #127's own pull request the previous evening. **What decided it was the record, not one run -- and my first reading overstated it.** I re-ran the last v4.39.3 commit (`85aa801`) as a control, it came back green, and I wrote that v4.39.4 fails. Then `analyze` PASSED on `main` at `340d243`, on v4.39.4. One pass against one fail proves nothing; the run history does: on 2026-11-25 every one of 25 CodeQL runs on v4.39.3 was green, and 2 of the 5 runs on v4.39.4 (counting #127's own pull request) failed, both at the same step. So v4.39.4 is intermittently broken, not deterministically, and the PR's green and `340d243`'s green are the same flake landing the other way. The log shows where: the analysis completes, then v4.39.4 runs "Combining SARIF files using the CodeQL CLI" over the job's two language runs (python, actions), warns "Column kind mismatch between SARIF files", and the upload step fails with no message. GitHub has documented that code scanning stopped combining multiple SARIF runs uploaded in one file. A single-run upload is unaffected -- `scorecard.yml`'s `upload-sarif` stays on v4.39.4 and its check is green -- so only `codeql.yml`'s `init` and `analyze` move back, together, with the reason written beside them. `.github/dependabot.yml` ignores that one release only, so the next one is still proposed. Because the failure is intermittent, one green run on that pull request will not show it is fixed; take it after several. **The rule for the next merge:** "everything green" is every check, not the required eight. A non-required job is still a check, and a security scanner that cannot upload is a scanner whose results nobody sees. | `ci:analyze`, `.github/workflows/codeql.yml`, `.github/dependabot.yml` | G393 |
| G396 | **A Pod that named five objects none of which were in the input audited at exit 0, and nothing said so -- and the first repair fired HIGH on every correct GitOps repository in the suite.** A Pod naming a ConfigMap volume, a Secret volume, a PersistentVolumeClaim, a ServiceAccount and an `envFrom.configMapRef`, none present, reported one unrelated LOW finding (`KN-S014`) and 61 rules not evaluated, in a single file AND in a directory: the cause was not an abstention but an absent rule. `KN-C013` is about a pull secret in the WRONG namespace and `KN-C015` reads only ResourceClaims, so no rule asked the question, and the accounting invariant could not see that -- every rule was in a bucket. The first `KN-C017` fired on every referenced name a whole-namespace directory did not define. **The full suite answered with 98 failures, every one a correct repository**: 82 in `test_verify.py` (each `good/` directory went from allow to deny), the gate's own `good/` fixture (`Secret 'orders-db'`), the bundle `generate` writes (`Secret 'app-tls'`), a hardened Pod's `ServiceAccount 'ok'`, the renderers and the hook. All three distinct names were of a kind the namespace defined NONE of. **That is why the first design was wrong:** `cli._partial_input` takes a directory to be the whole namespace, which is sound for what people commit (a NetworkPolicy, a PodDisruptionBudget) and unsound for what they do not -- a correct GitOps repository commits no Secrets (sealed-secrets, external-secrets, cert-manager or a person create them) and often no ServiceAccounts or claims, so a gate that fired there would be turned off, a worse defect than the false pass. The rule now judges a (namespace, kind) only where the bundle defines at least one object of that kind there: a name with nothing behind it is then a real gap, most often a typo, and the message names the objects that ARE defined (`db-creds` beside a reference to `db-cred`) and says "unless something outside this bundle creates it", so it stays true where a producer the rules do not read made the Secret. A kind the namespace defines none of is not judged and the record says which (`notEvaluated`: *"the bundle defines no ConfigMap or PersistentVolumeClaim or Secret or ServiceAccount in namespace shop, so a name a pod spec gives there may be an object created outside the bundle ... and its absence is not evidence"*); a fragment abstains as before (*"part of a namespace was passed, so an object that lives in a file you did not pass is not an absent one"*). So the reproduction itself exits 0 again -- named under `notEvaluated`, not passed -- and the same Pod in a namespace that manages the four kinds fires five HIGH findings at exit 1. Secret stays in scope on evidence: the suite's two Secret false positives were both in namespaces defining no Secret, which the gate removes, and the defined-some-referenced-a-missing-one typo is the case a reader most needs caught; the residue -- a namespace that commits some Secrets while a producer makes others -- is answered by the message's "unless", because reading cert-manager, SealedSecret or ExternalSecret objects would mean declaring kinds outside `SUBJECT_GROUPS`, which `test_absent_kinds.py` refuses and which would change what every rule refuses. A rule that fires on one kind and does not judge another RAN, so it is partly evaluated, and `run_rules` gave such a rule's note from `ctx.declined`, which `nothing_to_judge` never writes: the note fell back to "a field in this input has a type it cannot read", false about this input; it reads `no_verdict_reasons` before that fallback now. Escape hatches unchanged and each tested with its kind MANAGED, so the hatch and not the abstention is what passes: `optional: true` on the six fields that take it, `default` and an unset `serviceAccountName`, a `claimName` a StatefulSet's `volumeClaimTemplates` stands for, an object of that kind and name the rules refused. Out of scope by design: cluster-scoped referents (a directory is a namespace, never the cluster) and `imagePullSecrets` (an absent one does not stop the Pod; `KN-C013`'s field). Eight mutants, each killed: the fragment branch, the firing, the four hatches, the engine note (one test each, and only that test), and deleting the managed-kind check, which brings back 82 + 4 + 2 + 1 + 3 + 1 of the first design's failures in `test_verify`, `test_a_file_nobody_read_is_not_a_pass`, `test_gate`, `test_generate`, `test_renderer_conformance` and `test_verify_hook`, plus seven of this file's. Mechanical: CM-6's "not evaluated" count on the refused-Deployment input 27 -> 28 (the new rule maps to CM-6) and the scorecard's rule count 98 -> 99, both regenerated or updated, no assertion weakened. In `cprules.py` (44,101 -> 57,386 bytes) because `rules.py` had 2,966 bytes below the 262,144-byte plugin cap; the engine note cost it 277 (259,455).
