(a) `Toolkit.mechanism` with the default `partial_bundle=True` and one pasted Deployment at `replicas: 1`: `broken`, exit 1, hop `more-than-one-replica` `fails`. KN-C007 fires where no HorizontalPodAutoscaler holds the workload at two, and on a fragment the autoscaler may be in the file nobody pasted; the same is true of KN-V011 ("matches no pod template in namespace ...") and KN-N012 ("selects none of the pod templates"), whose pod may be in another file. The CLI gave the same answer for `-f one-file.yaml`. `MechanismIn.partial_bundle` said the opposite: "with a fragment such a rule reaches no verdict, so the hop it judges says `cannot judge`". Not a false pass -- a false BROKEN, which is the product's rule pointed the other way: an absence read as a finding. **Which was wrong, the behaviour or the description: the behaviour, and it is the WALK's behaviour that changed, not the rules'.** The report proposed making the three rules decline on a fragment, as KN-N001 and KN-C008 do. They were left alone on purpose: for `audit` each finding is a true sentence about the objects in front of it ("this Deployment runs 1 replica(s)"), KN-C007 already carries "evaluated without HorizontalPodAutoscaler" in the ledger, and silencing them would take the two commonest findings out of every single-file audit. The walk makes a stronger claim than the audit -- BROKEN is a verdict about the path -- so the walk is where the claim is withheld. A lesson now names, per hop, the rules whose finding rests on an object that is not in the input (`absent:`; shipped as KN-C007, KN-V011 and KN-N012), `walk()` takes the `partial_bundle` the caller gave `analyze`, and on a fragment a hop whose EVERY finding is from an `absent` rule is `cannot judge`, with the findings still listed under it and the reason saying to pass the whole namespace. A finding from any other rule on the hop is BROKEN, fragment or not. The field's description was tightened to say exactly this (the rule declines, or the walk lists its finding without reading it as a verdict about the namespace). (b) `walk(lesson, {})` answered `complete`, exit 0, for a lesson whose only hop was `needs: [], rules: [KN-N001]`: on an empty input `analyze` returns before any rule runs, so `notEvaluated` is empty too and every rule reads as silent. Unreachable through the two shipped lessons, whose earlier hops stop first, and the first thing a third lesson would hit. | G405 | **Nothing read the link between an Ingress and the Service it routes to, so an Ingress routing to a Service that does not exist walked "A request finds your Pod" complete at exit 0 (review classes 1, 4 and 13).** Red-team finding M2, reproduced through the CLI on the shipped fixture with one edit each: the backend naming `ghost` (no such Service in the directory), and the backend naming port 9999 on a Service that serves 8080. Both printed five `holds` and `every observable hop on this path holds`, exit 0. The honest answer for a whole namespace is BROKEN: the controller has nowhere to send the request and answers 503. **The cause was an absent rule, not an abstention**, which is why the accounting invariant could not see it (the G396 shape): KN-N003, KN-N009, KN-N011 and KN-V019 read the Ingress alone, KN-V011 and KN-V012 read the Service against its pods, and the comment above KN-C017 that sent this question to "KN-V011 and KN-N009" named two rules that do not ask it. The lesson drew `edge-admits` and `service-selects-pods` as adjacent hops, so its title claimed a path where five unrelated per-kind facts had been checked. **The repair is the rule, not a disclaimer.** KN-V024 (VALIDATE, wiring, MEDIUM, subjects Ingress and Service) reads `spec.defaultBackend` and every `spec.rules[].http.paths[].backend`, and follows `service.name` and `service.port.number` or `.name` to the Services of the Ingress's own namespace. It lives at the end of `cprules.py`, because `rules.py` is 1.5 KiB under the plugin directory's 256 KiB per-file limit. It keeps KN-C017's discipline, for the reason G396 learned with 98 failures: a PORT is judged whenever the Service is in the input, fragment or not, because both objects are in front of the rule; a MISSING Service is a finding only where `partial_bundle` is false AND the namespace defines at least one Service -- a fragment cannot show an absence, and a namespace that commits an Ingress and no Service has its Services made elsewhere. Those two cases, a backend that is not a Service (`resource`), a named Service that declares no ports, and a backend naming no readable port are each recorded through `nothing_to_judge` with a fixed phrase: `notEvaluated`, never clean. A Service of that name the rules refused to read (a Knative `Service`) goes through `cannot_judge_absence`, so it is not called absent (G368). The network lesson gains the hop `edge-names-a-service-here` between the edge and the Service (`needs: [Ingress]`, `reads: [Ingress, Service]`), which is BROKEN on the two reported cases at exit 1 and `cannot judge` on a fragment whose Service was not passed. Registered where a rule that needs another object is registered: `RULE_CONTROLS` (the validation controls), the admission webhook's skipped rules, and the policy generator's reasons. **Not done:** `backend.serviceName` of the removed `extensions/v1beta1` shape is read as "not a Service"; and the rule does not follow a Gateway API `HTTPRoute` or an OpenShift `Route`, which already stop the walk for want of an Ingress. **Tests:** five ways of routing to nothing (no such Service, a port number it does not serve, a port name it does not have, one path of two, the default backend) each break the hop with one KN-V024 finding naming the Ingress; the link holds by number and by name; on a fragment a missing Service is no finding and the hop stops while a wrong port is still BROKEN; an Ingress in a namespace that defines no Service, a `resource` backend, a Knative Service of the same name and an input with no Service at all are each not judged and not clean; the CLI prints `2. [BROKEN]` and exits 1 on the report's two cases. | G401 | **A third mechanism lesson, RBAC and ServiceAccounts, written from measurements instead of from rule titles (review classes 9 and 13).** The two lessons before it each shipped a hop that claimed more than its rule decided -- KN-C009 assumed to judge an absent PodDisruptionBudget when it abstains (G389), six titles saying what no rule read (G395), KN-C007 never looking at the HPA floor (G397) -- so each of this lesson's six rules was run first, with `audit -f <dir> --format json` and again on the single file, on its object present and fine, present and violating, and ABSENT. **The table.** KN-S014: silent when the pod sets `automountServiceAccountToken: false`, or leaves it unset while its ServiceAccount is in the input and sets it false; fires when it is unset beside a ServiceAccount that says nothing, when the pod sets it true over a ServiceAccount that opts out, and -- the cell that shaped the lesson -- when it is unset and NO ServiceAccount object is in the input at all, or only another one is, from a directory and from one file alike; abstains with no pod spec. KN-S028 and KN-S016: silent on a binding to one ServiceAccount (KN-S028 also on a binding with no subjects), fire on `system:authenticated` and on `roleRef.name: cluster-admin` (RoleBinding and ClusterRoleBinding), abstain with no RoleBinding or ClusterRoleBinding in the input. KN-S015 and KN-S017: silent on `get` of one named ConfigMap and on a Role with no rules, KN-S015 fires on `*` in verbs, KN-S017 on `*` on secrets, on `get, list` on secrets and on `create` on `pods/exec` (where KN-S015 is silent), and both abstain with no Role or ClusterRole -- including when a RoleBinding that names one IS there. KN-S027: silent on an Opaque Secret, fires on type `kubernetes.io/service-account-token`, abstains with no Secret. Directory and single file agreed on every row. **What the measurements changed.** Five of six abstain on absence, so a missing binding, Role or Secret stops the walk at its hop (`cannot-judge`, exit 5) and never holds; the test removes each object in turn from the complete bundle and checks the hop, the hops before it, that none is drawn after it, and the code, under both `partial_bundle` values. KN-S014 is the exception and it errs the other way, the direction G394 found on the network lesson's policy hop: on one pasted Deployment it reports a pod for mounting the token of a ServiceAccount nobody passed, which may be the one that opts out. A hop resting on that rule alone is BROKEN at exit 1 on a fragment, so the hop declares `needs: [ServiceAccount]` and is `cannot judge` without one. The order changed too: the proposed path put the legacy token Secret second, and with no Secret in the input KN-S027 abstains even from a directory, so a bundle whose Secrets live in an external store would have stopped before a single RBAC hop was read. It is the last observable hop. **What the titles may say.** Each rule reads every object of its kind in the input and none joins a pod to its ServiceAccount, a ServiceAccount to its bindings or a binding to its role, so the proposed hop "the roles bound to it carry no wildcard grants" is not something any rule decides. The titles say "No Role or ClusterRole here ...", and a binding to the built-in `admin` ClusterRole beside an unrelated harmless Role leaves both Role hops holding -- true of what they claim -- with "a role a binding names that is not in this input was not read" in the sentence beside the verdict; the test pins the sentence and refuses a title containing "bound to" or "can do". The last hop, the API server authorizing the request, is `observable: false`: the authorizer chain, the built-in and aggregated ClusterRoles and the bindings of other namespaces are cluster state. **Unlike the network lesson, a single file walks as its directory does** -- none of these rules fires because another object is absent -- and the two JSON documents are compared whole. The command and the `learn_mechanism` tool are compared whole on the complete and broken bundles, as directory and as file, and on a stopped walk. **Mutations measured failing, restore measured green:** dropping `needs: [ServiceAccount]` (3 fail), making the walker ignore `notEvaluated` (3 fail, the no-pod-spec stop and a synthetic Role hop with no `needs`, so the stop does not rest on the YAML list alone), and turning the broken fixture's `*` into `get` (1 fails). **Left out, and why.** No hop says the ServiceAccount a pod names exists, that a binding's subject or role exists, or what a ServiceAccount can do: no rule on this path reads those joins and `rules.py` is at its size cap, so they are stated as limits, not added as rules. The first hop over-withholds in one case, measured: a pod that sets the mount off itself, with no ServiceAccount object in the input, is fully decided by KN-S014 and still stops, because `needs` is checked before the rule. And it under-withholds in one: with ANOTHER ServiceAccount in a fragment, KN-S014's finding on a pod whose own ServiceAccount was not passed still breaks the hop -- that is the audit's own answer on the same input, and repairing it is a change to the rule. | `src/kube_noob/data/curriculum.yaml`, `tests/test_rbac_walk.py`, `tests/fixtures/mechanism/rbac/complete/bundle.yaml`, `tests/fixtures/mechanism/rbac/broken/bundle.yaml` | G387, G389, G394, G395, G397, G400 |
| G411 | **A hop's explanation was cut at 200 characters with no mark, and the cut fell on its caveat (review class 13).** Found by reading the RBAC lesson's first walk: the text view's `_line` bounded every value at 200 characters, which is right for a name out of the input and wrong for the curriculum's own `holds` sentence -- whose last clause is where each hop states what its rule does not read ("KN-S014 does not read a projected serviceAccountToken volume", "a role of the same reach under another name is not what KN-S016 reads"). Mutation-proved: with the `<` escape removed from the embedded JSON, with `html.escape` removed, and with the HTML branch returning 0, the escaping and exit-code tests fail; restored, all pass. `--format html` on any other command is refused at exit 2, as `mermaid` is outside `posture reach`, rather than printing YAML under the name (`tests/test_a_walk_you_can_watch.py::test_html_is_refused_where_there_is_no_walk_and_the_other_formats_still_work`). | G430 | **The page of a mechanism walk was a grid of boxes with three buttons, and looked nothing like the lesson player it was meant to follow (review classes 6, 9 and 13).** G402 drew `learn mechanism --format html` as a schematic: one bordered box per hop, "Previous hop / Play / Next hop", and a banner. It is now a port of KubeLearn's lesson player (MIT, Copyright (c) 2026 Joe Sabbagh), driven by the same dict `walk()` returns: a stage with an isometric scene, a caption strip and a transport bar; a 360px story column ("Hop n of N", the hop's title and whole explanation, a facts table of object, rules, verdict and findings, then the list of hops); a Cluster / Sequence toggle; an Events drawer of the evidence; dark and light themes; and an overview as step 0 that replaces the banner (path verdict, exit code and its meaning, the four counts, the walk's reason, the namespace judged). The numbering skips G421-G429 on purpose: another branch is adding rows there. **The scene stands on the reader's own objects.** A hop that names an object stands on a card with the kind's badge (ING, SVC, DEP, NP, SA, ROLE, RB, HPA, STS, PVC and the rest), the object's name and its namespace; two hops that read one object share its card and the card shows the worse of them; a kind this build has no badge for gets its capitals, and a kind out of the input is never a class name (`tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_the_scene_has_one_card_per_object_a_hop_names`, `tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_a_card_two_hops_stand_on_shows_the_worse_of_them`, `tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_a_kind_this_build_has_no_badge_for_gets_its_capitals_and_never_markup`, `tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_two_different_objects_at_one_spot_of_a_layout_do_not_stand_on_each_other`). **A thing no manifest holds is drawn as one.** The visitor's laptop, cluster DNS, the node's packet path, the scheduler, the API server and the worker nodes drawn as scenery are KubeLearn's external and node actors, each with a dashed outline and the words "not in any manifest"; scenery has no hop, so it has no verdict (`tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_what_no_manifest_holds_is_dashed_and_says_so`, `tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_the_request_lesson_draws_its_unobservable_last_hop_as_a_node`). **A verdict is a glyph and a word wherever it is shown** -- a tick only beside a hop that holds, a cross and BROKEN, `?` and cannot judge, a hollow ring and "not checked by any manifest", and "not walked" -- on the card, in the list, in the facts, in the sequence view and in the drawer. KubeLearn colours a Service card green; here no kind of object is green, because a card with no verdict yet must not look like a pass (`tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_a_broken_hop_carries_the_cross_and_the_word_on_its_card_and_in_the_list`, `tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_every_state_in_the_list_is_a_glyph_and_a_word_and_only_a_hold_gets_the_tick`, `tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_green_is_kept_for_a_hop_that_holds`). **A stopped walk greys what it did not walk.** The hops after a stop are drawn as not walked from the start, on cards of their own and never on the card of a walked hop that reads the same kind; this view capped at 600), the list and the evidence table are in the HTML as written, and the scene is drawn in its final state (`tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_without_javascript_the_page_lists_every_hop_its_verdict_and_its_whole_explanation`, `tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_an_explanation_longer_than_the_old_cap_is_on_the_page_whole`, `tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_the_overview_is_step_zero_and_says_what_the_exit_code_means`, `tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_the_drawer_lists_the_evidence_the_walk_recorded`). **Every lesson is drawn.** The four shipped lessons have a layout by role (edge actors front right, traffic cards in front of the nodes, workloads and policy behind), a test fails when a walkable lesson has none, and a lesson with no layout -- or a walk whose hops are not its lesson's -- is drawn as a plain isometric row rather than refused (`tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_every_lesson_renders_on_its_fixtures_the_same_twice_and_small`, `tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_the_walks_reach_every_exit_code_and_every_state`, `tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_a_lesson_with_no_layout_is_drawn_as_a_row_and_not_refused`). **What G402 held still holds, unchanged**: no URL on the page (not even the ported project's), the same Content-Security-Policy, the same bytes for the same walk, nothing from a manifest as markup -- now in SVG text too -- and the walk's exit code in every format (`tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_a_hostile_object_name_is_text_in_the_scene_and_in_the_sequence`, `tests/test_a_walk_you_can_watch.py::test_the_page_names_no_address_and_forbids_fetching_one`, `tests/test_a_walk_you_can_watch.py::test_no_string_from_the_walk_becomes_markup_or_a_command`, `tests/test_a_walk_you_can_watch.py::test_every_format_exits_with_the_walks_exit_code`). the marker's chip is moved off any actor label it would cover; the card the walk is standing on shows THAT hop's verdict, with an earlier break on the same card kept on its second line as `hop 1` and a cross, while the overview and the page without the script show the worse of the two; a card for a kind the walk names no object for takes the name its findings give it (one object: that object; several: the first and how many more; another kind's object is not borrowed), and the Object fact says the name came from the finding; and a hop that stays on the card of the hop before it is captioned `02/07  at Ingress/shop/shop`, not as a journey from an object to itself (`tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_the_camera_on_a_hop_crops_no_actor_and_keeps_the_caption_band_clear`, `tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_the_chip_on_the_marker_is_moved_off_a_label_it_would_cover`, `tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_the_chip_is_clear_of_every_label_on_the_shipped_walks`, `tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_the_card_under_the_walk_shows_this_hops_verdict_and_keeps_an_earlier_break_beside_it`, `tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_a_card_for_a_kind_takes_the_name_the_evidence_gives_it`, `tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_several_objects_in_the_evidence_are_counted_and_another_kind_is_not_borrowed`, `tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py::test_a_hop_that_stays_on_its_card_is_at_it_and_not_a_journey_from_it_to_itself`). **Not held by a test**: how the page looks. It was compared by eye with KubeLearn's player at 1440x800 dark and light and at 390x844, and the marker's flight, the camera and the keys were driven in headless Chromium, on the day it was written; no test opens a browser. | `src/kube_noob/learn/mechanism_html.py`, `src/kube_noob/learn/mechanism_scene.py`, `src/kube_noob/learn/mechanism_player.py`, `src/kube_noob/learn/iso.py`, `tests/test_the_walk_like_kubelearn.py`, `tests/test_a_walk_you_can_watch.py`, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` | G402, G411, G420 |

| G423 | **A walkable lesson had no way to say which bundles show it, so every consumer listed them by hand.** The website drew each mechanism lesson beside example bundles hard-coded on its side; a lesson added here (G420, G421, G422) had none there until someone remembered, and nothing in this repository knew which fixture belongs to which lesson. **The repair** is an optional `examples:` list on a lesson -- fixture directories relative to the repository's test fixtures -- set on all six walkable lessons, and `mechanism.examples()` to read it. It is read as strictly as a step: a non-empty list of distinct relative paths made of lowercase segments, so `..`, a leading or trailing `/`, a backslash, an uppercase letter, a newline, a dot-file and an address all raise, and `walk` calls the reader so a lesson with a malformed field is refused rather than walked. The walk's answer does not carry the field and the text view does not print it: the wheel ships no tests, and a path the installed package does not have is not an answer. **Found while writing it**: `mech-cp-a-node-goes-dark` has no example of its own -- it borrows the request-path bundles, and both are BROKEN at the spread hop and then stop at the budget hop for want of a PodDisruptionBudget. That is recorded in the table, not repaired here. **Tests** write out every (lesson, example, exit code) and fail by name on a lesson without examples or an example whose verdict moved; each example must be a directory holding a manifest whose walk reaches `complete` or `broken`; seventeen malformed values are refused by the reader and by the walk; a lesson without the field still walks; and the walk's JSON and text carry no example path. | G431 | **A pod spec with zero regular containers was read as clean (review classes 1 and 24; P0 of the learning-expansion triage).** The API server refuses a pod with no regular container (`spec.containers: Required value`), and init and ephemeral containers do not count toward it. The bundled OpenAPI schema says only that the `containers` KEY is required, so `containers: []` validated; every container rule then iterated nothing and was recorded evaluated and clean. **Measured before the change** with `analyze` on ten kinds and seven pod specs: `containers: []`, alone or beside an init or an ephemeral container, produced no validation finding on Pod, Deployment, StatefulSet, DaemonSet, ReplicaSet, ReplicationController, Job, CronJob and PodTemplate, where a missing or null key produced KN-V007; DeploymentConfig and RayCluster, which have no bundled schema that requires the key, were silent on all six shapes. Five of the six mechanism lessons walked their own `complete` example, emptied of containers, at exit 0 or held a workload hop on it -- `mech-cp-deploy-an-app` printed that every container names its image by digest and declares a readiness probe about none. **The repair** is in the schema stage, where the missing key already is, and under the same id (KN-V007, `validate`, category `schema`, high), not a rule a caller can leave out: `schemas.validate._no_regular_container` reports every PodSpec `pod_templates` reads whose `containers` is an empty list, and one whose key is absent where no schema reported that already, so one fact is one finding. A KServe component is not judged (`containers` is optional there; a predictor is `model:`), an object the rules refused is not judged a second time, and a `containers` of the wrong type stays the schema's. Because the finding is a schema one, G406 already stops a walk from holding a hop on the object: the five lessons now stop at exit 5 on the first hop that reads the workload. `mech-net-network-policy` still walks `complete`, because none of its hops reads a workload kind -- pinned, not repaired. **Tests** pin the 77-cell table and walk each lesson's emptied example; the row in `tests/test_deploy_an_app_walks.py` that pinned the gap now pins the finding. | G432 | **KN-N007 passed an ingress rule that names ports and no `from`, which admits every source on those ports (review class 1; P0 of the learning-expansion triage, and the limit G421 wrote into its hop).** `from` is optional, and an absent or empty one matches all sources. The rule read "no peers" and then asked whether the rule also named no ports, so naming a port was read as naming a peer: `ingress: [{ports: [{port: 8080}]}]` was evaluated and clean, the NetworkPolicy lesson held its hop on it with a sentence admitting as much, and the ValidatingAdmissionPolicy generated for the rule carried the same clause. **Measured first**, one Deployment and one policy in a whole namespace: `{}`, `from: []` and `ports: []` fired; `ports` with no `from`, with `from: []` and with `from: null` did not (the brief's "already fires on `from: []`" is true only without ports); `namespaceSelector: {}` and `0.0.0.0/0` fired on the peer; `from: [{podSelector: {}}]` is silent and stays a stated limit. **The repair**: an absent or empty `from` is a finding with or without ports, against the policy, at the rule's path, and the message names the ports (the first five, cleaned, then a count). The generated policy's else-branch is `false`. The hop's sentence now names the remaining limit (`podSelector: {}`), and the repository's own `examples/verify-web.yaml` -- which carried exactly this rule and would have turned the verify demo's "fixed manifest is allowed" into a deny -- names a peer. **The mirrored egress case was measured and belongs to no rule today**: `egress: [{}]`, `to: []`, `ports` with no `to` and `to: 0.0.0.0/0` are all silent on every KN-N rule. KN-N007's title, description, controls and generated policy are about ingress; no rule owns open egress, so reporting only the ports-without-`to` shape would flag the narrowest open rule and pass the two wider ones; and `ports: [53]` with no `to` is what KN-N013 accepts as a route to DNS. It is pinned as silent rather than given an id, and the rule's description now says it does not read egress. **Tests** pin both tables, the message, a port list the message cannot read, and the audit against the generated policy under cel-python. Mutations: the old condition restored (6 fail), egress rules read as ingress (6), only a missing key counted (3), the old CEL clause restored (4). | `tests/test_an_ingress_rule_with_ports_and_no_from.py::test_the_ingress_table_still_holds`, `tests/test_an_ingress_rule_with_ports_and_no_from.py::test_the_mirrored_egress_shapes_are_in_no_rule_and_that_is_pinned`, `tests/test_an_ingress_rule_with_ports_and_no_from.py::test_the_generated_admission_policy_refuses_what_the_audit_reports`, `tests/test_an_ingress_rule_with_ports_and_no_from.py::test_a_port_list_the_message_cannot_read_is_still_a_finding`, `tests/test_a_packet_meets_a_network_policy.py::test_the_limits_the_rules_have_are_written_into_the_hops_that_hold`, `tests/test_a_packet_meets_a_network_policy.py::test_the_measurement_table_still_holds` | G421, G431 |
| G433 | **Three rules read a HorizontalPodAutoscaler and none judged the autoscaler itself, and each matched its target with its own copy of the same line (review classes 1, 9 and 24; P0 of the learning-expansion triage).** KN-C007 (`_has_hpa`), KN-C012 and KN-C014 each compared `scaleTargetRef`'s name, kind and namespace inline. **Measured before the change**, one Deployment and one HPA in a namespace: `minReplicas: 6` above `maxReplicas: 5`, which the API server refuses, produced nothing and EXCUSED KN-C007's single replica; an HPA whose target is only in another namespace, one whose target is nowhere, one naming `extensions/v1beta1` for an `apps` Deployment and one naming an Argo Rollout were all silent in every rule. **The repair is one resolver and one rule.** `rules.hpa_scales(ctx, hpa, doc, anywhere=False)` is the only place a scale target is matched, and all four rules call it; the three older rules answer what they answered, pinned on 26 shapes whole and as a fragment (the full reports were compared byte for byte before and after the refactor). **KN-V025** (`validate`, wiring, high): a floor above the ceiling is a finding on every HPA, the default floor of 1 included; a target of the same kind and name that this bundle defines in another namespace and not in the HPA's own is a finding, since a HorizontalPodAutoscaler scales only a target in its namespace. **A target that is in no namespace of the input is NOT EVALUATED, never a pass and never a finding**: the reason names the kind and says it may be created outside the bundle, through `nothing_to_judge`, so the score stands. On a fragment nothing is concluded from an absence. A refused object of the target's name in the HPA's namespace is not an absent one (G368), and withholds the other-namespace finding. **An apiVersion group mismatch is not evaluated**: the controller resolves by group and kind, which groups serve a kind depends on the cluster, and the shared resolver deliberately does not compare groups so that KN-C007 does not change -- so KN-V025 neither calls that object the target nor calls the target missing. A ref with no apiVersion is read as the other three rules read it; that it fails on a real cluster is no rule's finding yet, and is pinned. **A refused HPA** is the subject gate's and the evidence check's, as for KN-C012 (`_EVIDENCE_KINDS`): alone, the rule is not evaluated and the verdict undetermined; beside a readable HPA the rule is declined on it by name. The neighbour one key over had the same unguarded read: `dependencies: 3` raised TypeError. A `Chart.yaml` or `values.yaml` that is not UTF-8 or not YAML is a refusal in one line that names the file, never a parser's traceback. | G444 | **A walk that does not descend an entry and does not mention it has stated a verdict about a directory it did not finish (review classes 1 and 9; G332's sentence, failing on the one entry type the walk never sees).** A ConfigMap beside `sub -> ../outside`, where `outside` holds a privileged hostPID Pod: `audit -f` scored 100.0 at exit 0, `gate` printed "gate passed", `verify` answered `allow`, `audit --oscal` reported five objectives `satisfied`, and `posture scan --require-complete` printed "0 attack paths" at exit 0 in front of an internet-facing LoadBalancer. Four walks each built their own list -- `[f for f in sorted(d.rglob("*")) if f.is_file()]` twice, `os.walk` twice -- and none had a word for a symbolic link to a directory: `rglob` does not enter one, and the link itself fails `is_file()`. A dangling `broken.yaml -> nowhere.yaml` was dropped the same way by `audit` and `gate` while `verify` called it not-determined: three commands, two answers. **The decision, made once in `gate.walk` and asked by every walk: a link is never followed out of the directory that was named, and one that leaves it is named and withholds the verdict.** Refusing rather than following, for three reasons: following is what makes a walk unbounded and lets it loop; a directory handed to a gate is often somebody else's pull request, and `sub -> /` would have a read-only tool read the machine it runs on; and every other reader in this codebase that meets a link already refuses it (`verify`, the chart renderer, the operator reader, the must-gather reader), so following here would have been the exception. A link that resolves INSIDE the directory named is another name for something the walk reads under its real name: nothing is lost and nothing is said, which is what keeps a ConfigMap volume mount (`key -> ..data/key`, `..data -> ..<timestamp>`) at one copy of each file, and makes `loop -> .` harmless. A link to a directory that resolves OUTSIDE is always a gap, without looking inside to see whether it holds a manifest, because looking is following. A link to a FILE is one bounded read of the file it names and is still read by `audit` and `gate` (its findings are in the verdict, so nothing about it is silent); `verify` alone keeps refusing those, because it answers for `kubectl apply`. A link that points at nothing is a gap when it is named like a manifest and is not otherwise: a dangling `node_modules/.bin/x` in a checkout nobody installed is not unread input, and a gate held incomplete by it is one every reader turns off (G332). A directory that cannot be listed is a gap too -- `os.walk` drops it unless given an `onerror`. Every gap is a `skipped` row with `forbidsVerdict` in the gate and an `unreadable` row in the audit family, so the existing renderers carry it and the exit is 5; `--skip-unreadable` accepts it for `audit` as it does any unread file, and for `gate` and `verify`, which take several `-f`, a link into another directory named in the same command is read there and is not a gap -- which is what makes the message's own remedy ("name the directory it points at with -f as well") work; the first version printed that remedy and then reported the link anyway. `verify` reported a file link under the name of its target (`nowhere.yaml`, a file that is not there to open) and now names the link. Applied to: `audit`, `validate`, `redteam`, `fix`, `ksi`, `gate`, `verify`, `posture scan|snapshot|reach`, `vuln threat`, `policy migrate-check`, and the operator reader. **Not changed, and said:** the VEX directory reader, the dependency-file scan under `code`, the build-context walk and the must-gather reader have walks of their own with their own rules for a link. | `tests/test_nothing_in_the_input_is_skipped_silently.py::test_a_link_to_a_directory_outside_the_one_named_is_not_a_clean_directory`, `tests/test_nothing_in_the_input_is_skipped_silently.py::test_posture_scan_does_not_report_zero_paths_past_a_link`, `tests/test_nothing_in_the_input_is_skipped_silently.py::test_a_dangling_link_named_like_a_manifest_is_not_a_clean_directory`, `tests/test_nothing_in_the_input_is_skipped_silently.py::test_the_gate_report_carries_the_link_for_every_renderer`, `tests/test_nothing_in_the_input_is_skipped_silently.py::test_a_link_that_stays_inside_the_directory_is_another_name_and_not_a_gap`, `tests/test_nothing_in_the_input_is_skipped_silently.py::test_naming_the_directory_a_link_points_at_is_the_remedy_the_message_gives`, `tests/test_nothing_in_the_input_is_skipped_silently.py::test_a_real_subdirectory_is_still_read`, `tests/test_nothing_in_the_input_is_skipped_silently.py::test_a_configmap_volume_mount_is_still_one_copy_of_each_file`, `tests/test_nothing_in_the_input_is_skipped_silently.py::test_a_link_that_cannot_be_a_manifest_and_points_nowhere_is_not_reported`, `tests/test_nothing_in_the_input_is_skipped_silently.py::test_every_walk_asks_the_one_function`, `tests/test_nothing_in_the_input_is_skipped_silently.py::test_a_directory_that_cannot_be_listed_is_named`, `tests/test_renderer_conformance.py::test_renderer_conformance`, `tests/test_renderer_conformance.py::test_a_file_that_was_not_read_is_named_by_the_renderers` | G332, G350, G351 |
| G445 | **A chart nobody rendered is every workload it deploys, unread; it was filed with the CI workflows, as a file that is read, understood and holds no object (review classes 1 and 6; the decision `docs/report-ir.md` left open, answered).** A directory holding one hardened Deployment and `chart/templates/dep.yaml` -- `privileged: true` under `{{ .Release.Name }}` -- gave `gate -f dir` "## kube-noob gate passed" at exit 0 with the template under "Skipped", `verify -f dir` `allow`, and nothing at all under the pre-commit hook's `--quiet`; `SKIP_REASONS` said `("a Helm template:", False)`. Pointed at the chart itself, `gate -f chart/` said "nothing to check" at exit 2 and `audit -f chart/` exited 5 on "while constructing a mapping (line 4)", a YAML parser's answer to a file that is not YAML yet: one input, two codes, and neither named Helm. Now, in every command that walks a directory, a file under the `templates/` of a chart is unrendered input: it is named, the reason carries the exact command with the chart's own directory as the caller would type it (`kube-noob gate --chart <dir>`, `kube-noob helm-audit <dir>`, or `helm template <release> <dir>` piped to `gate -f -`; the outermost chart when the template is a subchart's), and no verdict is stated -- exit 5, `incomplete` and never `passed` in the gate report and so in its Markdown, SARIF, code quality file, HTML page and OSCAL. The decision is one function, `gate.unrendered_reason`, asked by the gate's walk, `verify`'s and the audit family's, and it is asked before YAML is. **`--chart` is the waiver, and it is one by doing the work:** when the same run renders the chart a template belongs to, the template is still listed, as audited through its render, and withholds nothing; rendering a different chart excuses nothing. A chart directory given alone exits 5 with that message, not 2: "nothing to check" is a usage error, and a chart is something to check that was not read. Kept from G354: `verify` still audits a file under `templates/` that is a manifest as it stands, because `kubectl apply -R -f` would send it. Two tests asserted the defect -- that the reason does not forbid a verdict, and that `verify` allows over a template that is not yet YAML -- and are inverted. The guard that every skip reason decides whether it forbids a verdict read only a bare returned string literal, which was one reason of five; when that one became an f-string it found none, so it now reads every reason the classifier and the functions it asks can return, and fails below five. **This turns a gate over a whole repository that holds a chart from green to exit 5 until the chart is rendered with `--chart`;** that is the point of the change and it is the first thing the changelog says. | | G446 | **A file that rewrites the objects beside it was read as one more object (review classes 1 and 3).** A hardened Deployment beside a `kustomization.yaml` whose inline patch adds `privileged: true`: the gate passed at exit 0, `verify` answered `allow`, `audit -f` on the Kustomization alone scored 100.0, and the only trace was "No bundled schema for Kustomization; structural checks skipped". `kustomize build` deploys a privileged container. A Rollout made the verdict incomplete; the one file that says the objects beside it are not what is deployed did not. With `apiVersion` and `kind` left out, which kustomize permits, the gate called the file "not a Kubernetes manifest" -- the silent class -- and passed, and `audit` reported KN-V001 on it: the wrong reason, and nothing about the overlay. A kustomization is unrendered input, and it reaches a command in two shapes, so it is caught in two places. **Written as a document** (`kustomize.config.k8s.io`, with a `kind`): the audit lists it under `notAudited` with the reason and `kubectl kustomize <dir>`, so the verdict is undetermined in every renderer that already carries that row -- exit 1 from `audit`, `validate`, `fix`, `ksi` and `gate` (headline `incomplete`), exit 5 from `verify` and from `posture scan --require-complete`, whose inventory now names it as `unmodelled` because that scan withholds a pass for exactly the objects the audit does not audit (G370); OSCAL reports no objective `satisfied`; the MCP tools get the same row through the same engine. The document is deliberately NOT declined by the directory walk: KN-C017 reads its `secretGenerator` and `configMapGenerator` to tell a generated Secret from a missing one (G410), and declining the file turned a correct overlay into a false finding -- the first version did that, and `test_the_directory_reader_sees_kustomization_yaml` caught it. An accepted `notAudited` exception accepts it by name, like any object nothing read. **Not a document** -- no `apiVersion` or `kind`, YAML that does not parse, or kustomize's third file name `Kustomization`, which has no extension and is opened by no walk: known by its name, as kustomize finds it, declined by the walk with `kubectl kustomize <dir>` piped to `gate -f -` and the directory as the caller would type it, and no verdict is stated (exit 5 in every command that walks a directory, through the same function and rows as G445). Giving the rendered output is what lifts either: a render holds no Kustomization. The other direction: Flux's `kustomize.toolkit.fluxcd.io` Kustomization is a cluster object applied as it stands, whatever its file is called, and withholds nothing. **Measured and left as their existing contract for an object nothing read:** `redteam`, `vuln threat`, `posture snapshot` and `posture scan` without `--require-complete` or `--fail-above` exit 0 and name the Kustomization in their output (the snapshot in the file it writes, not in the line it prints). | `tests/test_nothing_in_the_input_is_skipped_silently.py::test_a_kustomization_in_a_directory_withholds_the_verdict`, `tests/test_nothing_in_the_input_is_skipped_silently.py::test_the_commands_that_do_not_fail_on_an_unread_object_still_name_the_kustomization`, `tests/test_nothing_in_the_input_is_skipped_silently.py::test_the_kustomization_stays_in_the_input_for_the_rule_that_reads_its_generators`, `tests/test_nothing_in_the_input_is_skipped_silently.py::test_a_kustomization_that_is_no_document_is_declined_by_the_walk`, `tests/test_nothing_in_the_input_is_skipped_silently.py::test_a_kustomization_is_one_by_its_file_name_as_kustomize_reads_it`, `tests/test_nothing_in_the_input_is_skipped_silently.py::test_a_flux_kustomization_is_a_cluster_object_and_not_a_kustomize_file`, `tests/test_nothing_in_the_input_is_skipped_silently.py::test_a_kustomization_handed_over_as_text_withholds_the_verdict`, `tests/test_nothing_in_the_input_is_skipped_silently.py::test_a_kustomization_named_on_its_own_is_not_a_score_of_100`, `tests/test_nothing_in_the_input_is_skipped_silently.py::test_the_rendered_output_is_audited_like_any_manifest`, `tests/test_nothing_in_the_input_is_skipped_silently.py::test_every_reason_for_unrendered_input_forbids_a_verdict`, `tests/test_a_name_with_nothing_behind_it.py::test_the_directory_reader_sees_kustomization_yaml`, `tests/test_renderer_conformance.py::test_renderer_conformance` | G272, G370, G410, G445 |
| G440 | **The webhook asked the object whether to look at it, and the requester writes the object (review classes 1 and 9).** Red-team finding 2: with `admission.enforce: [KN-S001, KN-S009]`, `r_bad.json` (a privileged Pod) was denied and `r_owned.json` -- the same Pod with `ownerReferences: [{kind: ReplicaSet, name: anything, controller: true}]` -- was `allowed: true` with no warning; so was `r_owned_cm.json`, whose owner is a ConfigMap. G73 recorded "skips system and owned objects" and never asked who writes the owner field: the API server does not check that an owner controls anything, so whoever may create a Pod may write it, and on Kubernetes 1.30+ the chart's `not-owned-by-a-controller` matchCondition kept the request from arriving at all. The skip exists for a real reason -- a Deployment's findings would otherwise be reported three times, on it, its ReplicaSet and every Pod. **Two fixes were on the table and the choice is the one that keeps existing workloads running.** (a) Never skip while a rule is enforced, de-duplicating only in warn mode: every Pod of a Deployment that predates the rule would then be denied when its node drains, which is the freeze the UPDATE rule (deny only a finding the old object did not have) was written to prevent, moved to where no one sees it -- a `FailedCreate` event on a ReplicaSet; and it makes the two modes disagree about which requests are judged. (b) Skip on who SENT the request. `request.userInfo` is written by the API server from the authenticated identity, which is the one part of the review the requester does not control. Chosen: a Pod, ReplicaSet or Job is skipped only when the username is one of `admission.CONTROLLER_USERS` (kube-controller-manager's replicaset, statefulset, daemon-set, job, deployment and cronjob controllers, or the manager's own identity when it runs without per-controller credentials) AND a `controller: true` owner is one of `CHECKED_OWNERS` in `apps/v1` or `batch/v1` -- kinds the webhook is sent, so the owner really was judged. One rule for both modes: a person's object is judged whatever it says, a controller's child is not judged twice. Holding one of those identities takes a `kube-system` ServiceAccount token, the manager's certificate or impersonation rights, each of which already is the cluster. **What the old test also let through and this closes**: `replication-controller` is deliberately not trusted, because a ReplicationController is not a kind the webhook is sent, so a user-created one had its Pods skipped with nothing judged; and an operator's ServiceAccount is not trusted, because its custom resource was never judged either (G234's lesson, one layer up). Their Pods are judged now -- a duplicate for a DeploymentConfig, the first look for the rest. **The namespace skip, by the same question.** `request.namespace` is the API server's, but who may NAME a namespace `openshift-x` depends on the platform: OpenShift refuses the prefix to project requests, plain Kubernetes reserves nothing, so `r_kubesystem.json` (namespace `openshift-anything-i-made-up`, platform kubernetes) was admitted unjudged. `openshift` and `openshift-*` are skipped only on a cluster that IS OpenShift, and **the source of truth for that is the cluster's own API list, not the platform value**. The first form of this fix keyed on `config.defaultPlatform` alone, and a review found the hole still open: `openshift` is the chart's DEFAULT, so a default install on plain Kubernetes declared it and went on skipping `openshift-x` -- a default nobody chose is not evidence of anything (the same sentence as G441's `failurePolicy`). So two facts are required: the platform is in `OPENSHIFT_FAMILY`, and the cluster serves `config.openshift.io/v1` -- `.Capabilities.APIVersions`, which `helm install` and `upgrade` read from the cluster; `config.openshift.io` because it is the cluster-configuration group every OpenShift serves and no Route or SCC add-on does. The chart gates both the `namespaceSelector` entry and the `not-an-openshift-namespace` matchCondition on the conjunction and writes the result into the server's configuration as `admission.openshiftNamespacesReserved`. **The server cannot detect this itself** -- it is offline and read-only and, without `admission.lookups`, holds no credential -- so it does not try: `Config.openshift_reserved` defaults to False, which JUDGES those namespaces, and it skips only when told true and the platform agrees. `kube-noob admission review` with `defaultPlatform: openshift` and nothing else therefore denies `r_kubesystem.json`. The cost, stated: a chart rendered with `helm template` and no `--api-versions config.openshift.io/v1` judges the platform's namespaces on a real OpenShift -- the safe direction, and loud -- and NOTES.txt warns whenever the declared platform is OpenShift and the cluster does not serve the API. `kube-system`, `kube-public` and `kube-node-lease` stay exact names everywhere. `deletionTimestamp` skips on UPDATE only: the API server clears it on a create. **The chart is the policy, not the server**: a matchCondition is evaluated first, and an error in one skips the webhook under `failurePolicy: Ignore`, so the CEL reads the identity before it reads anything from the object, guards every optional field, and is evaluated in the test against the same matrix as the server, which must give the same answer for every case; the literals in the expression must equal the server's sets. The `admission-kind` job denies the forged Pod and a made-up `openshift-` namespace on a real API server, and deletes a Pod of a Deployment that already breaks the rule to see the controller's replacement admitted. Mutation-proved: ignoring the identity, the owner kind, the owner group, the platform, the evidence (in the server, in the chart, and in the command that carries it), the operation or the subresource, trusting `replication-controller`, and reading `controller` as truthy each fail a test here. **Not changed, and said in `docs/limits.md`**: a namespace labelled `kube-noob.io/admission=disabled` is skipped for whoever may label it; a rule that declines is a warning, not a denial (G337); the generated policies never skipped an owned object for a security rule and still skip one for calibrate rules (G59) | `tests/test_admission_cannot_be_bypassed.py::test_a_forged_owner_reference_does_not_skip_an_enforced_rule`, `tests/test_admission_cannot_be_bypassed.py::test_a_real_controllers_own_child_keeps_its_existing_behaviour`, `tests/test_admission_cannot_be_bypassed.py::test_neither_the_identity_nor_the_owner_is_enough_alone`, `tests/test_admission_cannot_be_bypassed.py::test_warn_mode_still_says_everything_about_a_forged_owner`, `tests/test_admission_cannot_be_bypassed.py::test_a_debug_container_on_a_managed_pod_is_judged_whoever_asks`, `tests/test_admission_cannot_be_bypassed.py::test_an_openshift_namespace_is_reserved_only_on_openshift`, `tests/test_admission_cannot_be_bypassed.py::test_a_platform_that_was_only_declared_reserves_nothing`, `tests/test_admission_cannot_be_bypassed.py::test_a_deletion_timestamp_on_a_create_does_not_skip`, `tests/test_admission_cannot_be_bypassed.py::test_the_charts_match_condition_skips_exactly_what_the_server_skips`, `tests/test_admission_cannot_be_bypassed.py::test_the_chart_reserves_openshift_namespaces_only_where_the_platform_does`, `tests/test_admission.py::test_update_denies_only_new_findings_and_skips`, `ci:Enforce mode - KN-S001 is denied, other findings warn, an existing breaker can still be updated` | G59, G73, G234, G250, G337 |
| G441 | **The answer was right and arrived after the API server had stopped waiting (review classes 1 and 13).** Red-team finding 3: through `admission.review_bytes` a Pod of 8,000 privileged containers (607 KB) took 4.37 s, 16,000 (1.22 MB) took 9.42 s and one container with 30,000 environment variables (1.01 MB) took 7.98 s; the chart's `timeoutSeconds` is 3 and `failurePolicy` was hard-coded `Ignore`, so each was admitted with no warning. G337 fixed "admitted what it could not evaluate, and said nothing" for the cases the webhook can see; a timeout is the one it cannot, and G73's "p99 under 50 ms" was measured on the rule fixtures and read as a property of the webhook. **Profiled first** (cProfile, 8,000 containers, 13.4 s under the profiler): 9.2 s was `yamlio.load_documents` -- `_findings` wrote the already-parsed object out with `json.dumps` and parsed it again with PyYAML's pure-Python composer, to build a line map in which every path is line 1 -- and 3.6 s was the rules, which are linear (G252). `yamlio.documents_from_objects` builds the same documents from the object, keeping what the round trip did do (the node budget and depth limit, lone surrogates replaced, list wrappers opened, the document cap, a fresh structure). Measured on the same bodies, before and after, build container: 16,000 containers 6.29 s and the 30,000-variable Pod 4.04 s before; with the second parse gone 2.2 s and 0.15 s. **Faster was not enough, so there is a budget, and it counts what costs.** 2.2 s is still past the deadline, and no rule work can be made free. Bytes are the wrong measure -- a megabyte of annotation is one value and is judged in milliseconds -- so the budget is `MAX_OBJECT_NODES`, 20,000 values (mappings, lists and scalars), applied to the object and to the old object of an update, after every skip and before any evaluation: the worst shape found costs about 26 microseconds a value, so an object at the budget takes about 0.5 s and an update about 0.9 s here. Past it, past `MAX_BODY`, when a rule raises, or when `Config.deadline` (three fifths of `timeoutSeconds`, which the chart now writes into the server's configuration) has passed before an evaluation would start, the answer is "not evaluated" at once: the megabyte Pods are answered in 0.04 s. The server evaluates on one worker thread and stops waiting at the deadline, answering with the request's own uid (any other is a failed call to the API server, which `Ignore` turns back into the silent admission); the event loop is no longer blocked, so probes answer during an evaluation, and a request still queued at its deadline is cancelled rather than evaluated late. `kube-noob admission review` passes no deadline, so the offline answer does not depend on the machine. **What "not evaluated" means is the decision.** In warn mode it is an admission with a warning that starts `kube-noob: not evaluated` -- warn mode's promise is that it never denies. With a rule enforced it is a denial with the reason and the enforced rules named: an object nobody judged has not passed, and every "...; admitted" path (too large, not JSON, not a review, a rule that raised on a shape the requester chose) was a way round `enforce`. The cost is stated: a legitimate object past the budget, or one a rule crashes on, cannot be created where a rule is enforced until its namespace is excluded. This replaces G73's "fails open" for enforce mode only; G337's choice for a kind no rule reads (admit, with a warning) is unchanged. **`failurePolicy` is what remains, and it is now the operator's.** When the webhook gives no answer at all the API server decides, and no value has both halves: Ignore = availability (admitted unjudged, with no warning anywhere), Fail = enforcement (refused, and a webhook that is down blocks). `admission.failurePolicy` defaults to `Ignore` -- the default mode is warn, where there is nothing to enforce, and an upgrade must not start blocking a cluster that had chosen nothing -- and `values.yaml`, the chart README, `docs/limits.md`, SECURITY.md and the install notes (when `enforce` is set with `Ignore`) say plainly that with `Ignore` the enforced rules are not enforced while the webhook is down. Not switched to `Fail` automatically when `enforce` is set: a default that changes with another value is one nobody chose. **Held**: the megabyte Pods through `review_bytes` against a ceiling derived from `Config.deadline` (`bounds.within_deadline`, refused as vacuous unless the times measured before are past it); the budget exact at its boundary and never outranking a skip; every unreadable-request path in both modes; the deadline checked before each evaluation with the clock moved by the test; the server answering at the deadline with the right uid while the evaluation is still held, and `/healthz` answering meanwhile; `documents_from_objects` equal to the round trip on every rule fixture -- data, findings, lines and what was not read -- because faster-by-skipping is the regression a clock rewards; time linear up to the budget, as a ratio. Mutation-proved: restoring the round trip, removing the node budget, counting only the new object, admitting on a raised rule or an oversized body in enforce mode, ignoring either deadline check, answering a timeout with an empty uid, evaluating on the event loop, and hard-coding `failurePolicy` each fail a test here. **Not done**: the chart was not rendered here (`helm` is not on this machine; `ci/helm-and-dogfood` and `admission-kind` do); the budget is a constant, not a value; the rules' own cost per finding (about 22 microseconds, a third of it re-deriving the object's name) is untouched | `tests/test_admission_answers_inside_its_timeout.py::test_a_megabyte_pod_is_answered_inside_the_webhooks_deadline`, `tests/test_admission_answers_inside_its_timeout.py::test_the_node_budget_is_exact_and_does_not_outrank_a_skip`, `tests/test_admission_answers_inside_its_timeout.py::test_a_request_the_webhook_cannot_read_is_not_admitted_as_if_it_had_passed`, `tests/test_admission_answers_inside_its_timeout.py::test_the_deadline_is_checked_before_each_evaluation`, `tests/test_admission_answers_inside_its_timeout.py::test_the_deadline_follows_the_charts_timeout`, `tests/test_admission_answers_inside_its_timeout.py::test_the_server_answers_at_its_deadline_while_the_evaluation_is_still_running`, `tests/test_admission_answers_inside_its_timeout.py::test_the_server_refuses_an_oversized_body_without_reading_it`, `tests/test_admission_answers_inside_its_timeout.py::test_the_documents_are_the_ones_the_round_trip_gave`, `tests/test_admission_answers_inside_its_timeout.py::test_the_webhook_does_not_parse_its_object_a_second_time`, `tests/test_admission_answers_inside_its_timeout.py::test_the_direct_path_keeps_the_parsers_limits_and_does_not_share_the_callers_object`, `tests/test_admission_answers_inside_its_timeout.py::test_the_time_is_linear_in_the_object_up_to_the_budget`, `tests/test_admission_answers_inside_its_timeout.py::test_failure_policy_is_a_choice_the_chart_states`, `tests/test_admission_answers_inside_its_timeout.py::test_the_chart_tells_the_server_how_long_the_api_server_waits`, `tests/test_admission.py::test_a_padded_object_does_not_outrun_the_webhooks_timeout`, `tests/test_admission.py::test_asgi_app_metrics_and_limits`, `tests/test_admission.py::test_p99_well_under_50_ms` | G73, G252, G263, G326, G337, G440 |
| G437 | **No page said what kube-noob's rules have to do with the DISA Kubernetes STIG, and an outside review that looked for one quoted a release two major versions old (review classes 1 and 9).** `docs/stig.md` is generated by `scripts/stig_crosswalk.py` from a pinned rule list (Version 2, Release 6, 92 rules: ids, rule ids, severities and titles, with the hash of the list and of the files it was parsed from, and a header that says it is a mirror because DISA's XCCDF could not be read) and a hand-written mapping in which every row has exactly one of four statuses. **Measured, not assumed**: every `covered` and `partly` row is run on fixtures -- the manifest the rule reports, the one it does not, and for `partly` the manifest that is DISA's finding and clean to kube-noob -- and ten CAT I rows marked `not covered` are run too, so the row fails the day a rule starts telling its finding from its absence. A row may not invent an id or drop one, a rule id may appear only on a row that claims coverage, a check that reads a node may only be `not determinable` and must name the evidence, and no status is a pass: KN-S038 and KN-S039 decline, and are not clean, when no static pod manifest is in the input. Mutation-proved: with KN-S038's `--anonymous-auth=true` finding removed the V-242390 proof fails, and with KN-S039's client-certificate finding removed the V-242423 proof fails. The counts in `docs/compliance.md` are guarded by `scripts/doc_numbers.py` from the same data. Not done: a `kube-noob` command or MCP tool over the crosswalk, and rules for the 43 backlog rows. | `tests/test_stig_crosswalk.py::test_the_snapshot_hashes_to_what_it_records`, `tests/test_stig_crosswalk.py::test_the_snapshot_pins_version_2_release_6_and_92_rules`, `tests/test_stig_crosswalk.py::test_every_v_id_is_unique_and_well_formed`, `tests/test_stig_crosswalk.py::test_every_row_is_a_snapshot_id_and_every_snapshot_id_has_a_row`, `tests/test_stig_crosswalk.py::test_no_status_other_than_the_four`, `tests/test_stig_crosswalk.py::test_every_rule_a_row_names_exists_and_only_judging_rows_name_one`, `tests/test_stig_crosswalk.py::test_a_check_that_reads_a_node_is_never_called_determinable`, `tests/test_stig_crosswalk.py::test_every_covered_and_partly_row_has_a_fixture_proof`, `tests/test_stig_crosswalk.py::test_a_judged_row_is_proved_on_a_fixture`, `tests/test_stig_crosswalk.py::test_no_rule_tells_an_uncovered_cat_i_finding_from_its_absence`, `tests/test_stig_crosswalk.py::test_a_static_pod_row_is_undetermined_when_no_static_pod_is_in_the_input`, `tests/test_stig_crosswalk.py::test_check_detects_drift_in_the_document_and_in_the_data`, `tests/test_stig_crosswalk.py::test_the_document_says_it_is_not_an_assessment`, `tests/test_stig_crosswalk.py::test_the_numbers_the_compliance_page_quotes_are_the_crosswalks_own`, `ci:STIG crosswalk matches the pinned rule list and the mapping` |
| G438 | **A benchmark of other people's charts is only worth publishing if every way of not having an answer stays in it (review classes 1 and 9).** `scripts/helm_benchmark.py` renders the 25 public charts pinned in `bench/charts.yaml` with helm, audits each with `kube-noob audit --platform openshift --version 4.22 --format json`, and writes `docs/benchmarks/helm-charts-openshift-4.22.md` and the JSON beside it; the `helm-benchmark` workflow runs it on `workflow_dispatch` only, because no helm and no chart repository is reachable from a development machine. **The rule:** every chart in the list has a row whatever happened to it. A chart that does not pull or does not render keeps helm's exit code and its words and is `pull failed` or `render failed`; one with no record of a render is `not rendered`; one whose output holds no Kubernetes object is `rendered nothing` with no score and no finding count, never a zero-finding row; an audit that exits with anything but 0 or 1, or whose output is not the audit document, is `audit did not answer`; kube-noob's own `undetermined` is kept and the kinds it had no reader for are named in that chart's row. No verdict is spelt clean or pass: the best one is `no findings in what was read`, beside the number of rules that had nothing to evaluate. Shares are out of the charts audited, never out of the list. The restricted-v2 share is stated as at least N, names the six rules it is read from (KN-S001, KN-S004, KN-S005, KN-S009, KN-S010, KN-S011: each fires only on a pod shape that SCC refuses) and lists apart the charts whose pods were not read. A version is one exact quoted X.Y.Z read from the repository's index or Artifact Hub and the list says which; a range or `latest` is refused. The first render is always the chart's defaults, and a `fallback` is used only after the defaults exit non-zero, with both attempts recorded and the reason printed. The date is an argument and never the clock, so the same directory gives the same bytes. What helm printed goes into `render.json` and, cut to one line with table and command characters removed, into the report; it is never echoed to the workflow log. The job that runs helm holds no write token; `open_pr` (default false) starts a second job that commits the two generated files and nothing else, and fails with the command to run when the pull request cannot be opened, saying in the pull request and in the log that one opened with `GITHUB_TOKEN` does not start CI. **Not measured here:** no chart has been pulled or rendered yet, the fallback values are expectations and not observations, and the workflow has no slowest green run to hold its 45-minute budget against. A ConfigMap alone scores 100 in kube-noob with 93 rules not evaluated; the report prints that count beside the score rather than change the score. | | G447 | **The megabyte that guards an MCP request was also the most `kube-noob audit -f` would read, so a chart with its CRDs in it could not be audited (review classes 1 and 9).** Four of the 25 charts in `bench/charts.yaml` -- argo-cd 10.20.0, cloudnative-pg 0.29.2, external-secrets 2.15.0 and kyverno 3.10.0 -- were `audit did not answer`: exit 4 and pydantic's `String should have at most 1000000 characters`, with a slice of the render quoted and a link to pydantic's site. `MAX_MANIFEST_CHARS` is `max_length` on the tools' models, and the command line builds the same models for local files. **Measured first** (`helm template bench <chart> --namespace bench --kube-version 1.35.0` on each chart's source at its tag): cloudnative-pg 1,307,969 bytes, argo-cd 2,133,868, external-secrets about 2,044,000 (its CRD bundle and the rest of the chart, which git does not hold as one render), kyverno 5,916,202 with the `kyverno-api` CRD chart at the pinned 0.0.1-alpha.5. On `main`: `audit`, `validate`, `fix`, `redteam` refused all four at the megabyte; `gate`, `verify` and `posture scan` read the first three and refused kyverno at a second limit of 5,000,000 that the YAML loader, the gate's walk, `verify` and the chart render each held separately. **The rule:** two limits for two kinds of caller, in one module (`kube_noob.limits`). One MCP or REST argument carries 1,000,000 characters, as before, with the same `maxLength` in every schema; one command-line run reads 16,000,000 bytes, all files together, which is 2.7 times the largest render and was measured at 53 s for `audit`, 103 s for `verify` and 361 MB at 15.3 MB of input. The request cap is the default; only `cli._input_limit` asks for the local one, not for `serve`, `mcp` or `admission serve`, and `tool_runner.call` sets the request cap again whatever its caller holds. The loader, the gate's walk, `verify`'s reader and `helm_render.MAX_RENDERED` are that one number. **Over either limit nothing is read**, and the refusal is one line with the size, the limit and what to do (`InputTooLarge`, a `ManifestError` and deliberately not a `ValueError`, so pydantic passes it through as itself): exit 4 on the command line -- `gate` and `verify` exited 2 for their 5 MB and now exit 4 like the rest -- a tool error over MCP, a 400 with outcome `invalid-arguments` over REST. The MCP SDK validates its own copy of the arguments first and reports a validator that raises anything but a ValidationError as a crash with no message, so that copy does not measure (`limits.deferred`) and the tool's own model does. **No limit cuts manifest text.** `insight` sliced manifests, a Containerfile and Kyverno policies to `[: MAX_MANIFEST_CHARS]` before auditing them: every surface caps `insight` at 400,000 characters so none could reach it, but `insight()` itself takes 2,000,000, and a caller of the function got an audit of the first megabyte listed under `ran`. The slices are gone -- the whole text is audited or the kind is under `skipped` with the reason -- and a text over `insight.MAX_TEXT` now carries `inputTruncated`. The tail cuts on logs and events (`diagnose`, `known-issues`, `build diagnose`, the CLI's `insight -f`) already report `inputTruncated` and are not manifest audits; they are unchanged. An argument a model refuses for any other reason is `argument: reason` (`errors.refused_arguments`) on the command line, the REST routes and the Agent Skill. The benchmark's row is `too large for the audit` when the exit code is 4 and stderr is a size refusal in any of the three wordings kube-noob has used, and `audit did not answer` otherwise. **Not done:** the MCP SDK's own report for an argument of the wrong type or an unknown name is still pydantic's text, because the SDK writes it; an argument that is over the cap only by trailing white space was refused by the SDK's copy and is now stripped and read, as the REST routes always did; the 1,000-document limit is unchanged and none of the four charts is near it (84 at most); the four charts were rendered from their git sources, not pulled, so the benchmark's own run is what confirms the row. Mutation proof: 36 deliberate breaks of the fix; 35 failed a test at once, and the 36th (the audit slice in `insight` put back) passed because a text cut mid-document does not parse, so the test now cuts exactly between two documents and fails on it. | `tests/test_large_input.py::test_a_render_over_a_megabyte_is_read_to_its_last_object`,
